The '''2016 United States Senate elections''' were held on November 8, 2016. The [[2016 United States presidential election|presidential election]], [[2016 United States House of Representatives elections|House elections]], [[2016 United States gubernatorial elections|14 gubernatorial elections]], and many [[2016 United States elections|state and local elections]] were held concurrently. In the elections, 34 of the 100 seats—all [[Classes of United States senators|Class 3 Senate seats]]—were contested in regular elections; the winners served six-year terms until January 3, 2023. Class 3 had last been up for election in [[2010 United States Senate elections|2010]] when Republicans won a net gain of six seats.

In 2016, Democrats defended 10 seats, while Republicans defended 24 seats. Republicans, having won a majority of seats in the Senate in [[2014 United States Senate elections|2014]], held the Senate majority with 54 seats before this election. Although the Democrats made a net gain of two seats, Republicans retained control of the Senate for the [[115th United States Congress]]. The two Democratic gains came from the defeats of incumbents [[Kelly Ayotte]] in [[2016 United States Senate election in New Hampshire|New Hampshire]] and [[Mark Kirk]] in [[2016 United States Senate election in Illinois|Illinois]] by [[Maggie Hassan]] and [[Tammy Duckworth]], respectively.

Despite Republicans retaining control of the Senate, 2016 marked the first time since [[1986 United States Senate elections|1986]] where Democrats made a net gain of seats in Class 3. This is also the only election cycle since the popular-vote election of senators was mandated by the [[Seventeenth Amendment to the United States Constitution|17th Amendment]] in 1913 that the winning party in every Senate election mirrored the winning party for their state in the presidential election.<ref>{{cite web | url=http://waunakeegan.weebly.com/waunakeegan-results.html | title=WaunaKeegan Results | website=WaunaKeegan | access-date=January 13, 2017 | archive-date=March 22, 2017 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170322074444/http://waunakeegan.weebly.com/waunakeegan-results.html | url-status=dead }}</ref><ref>[https://web.archive.org/web/20161111115202/http://fivethirtyeight.com/features/there-were-no-purple-states-on-tuesday/ There Were No Purple* States On Tuesday]. [[FiveThirtyEight]]. November 10, 2016.</ref> This feat had nearly been accomplished earlier in [[1920 United States Senate elections|1920]], which also involved the Class 3 Senate seats, and nearly repeated in [[2020 United States Senate elections|2020]]; in both cases, every state, with the exception of Kentucky in 1920 and [[2020 United States Senate election in Maine|Maine]] in 2020, voted for the same party in the presidential election and their Senate election. In addition, this election marked the first time since [[2000 United States Senate elections|2000]] in which the party in opposition to the elected or reelected presidential candidate made net gains in the Senate; both cases involved the election of a Republican president and the Democrats making gains in the Senate.

With the retirement of [[Harry Reid]], [[Chuck Schumer]] became the Democratic leader after the elections, while [[Mitch McConnell]] retained his position as [[Party leaders of the United States Senate|Senate Majority Leader]]. As of 2024, this is the last time Republicans won Senate races in [[Arizona]] and [[Georgia (U.S. state)|Georgia]], and the last senate election cycle where there were no special elections.

== Results summary ==
All 34 [[Classes of United States senators|Class 3 senators]] were up for election in 2016; Class 3 consisted of 10 Democrats and 24 Republicans. Of the senators not up for election, 34 of the senators not up for election were Democrats, 30 senators were Republicans, and two senators were independents who caucused with the [[Senate Democratic Caucus|Senate Democrats]].

== Final pre-election predictions ==
Several sites and individuals publish predictions of competitive seats. These predictions look at factors such as the strength of the [[incumbent]] (if the incumbent is running for reelection) and the other candidates, and the state's partisan lean (reflected in part by the state's [[Cook Partisan Voting Index]] rating). The predictions assign ratings to each seat, indicating the predicted advantage that a party has in winning that seat.

Most election predictors used:
* "<u>tossup</u>": no advantage
* "<u>tilt</u>" (used by some predictors): advantage that is not quite as strong as "lean"
* "<u>lean</u>": slight advantage
* "<u>likely" or "favored</u>": significant, but surmountable, advantage
* "<u>safe" or "solid</u>": near-certain chance of victory

Where a site gives a percentage probability as its primary indicator of expected outcome, the chart below classifies a race as follows:
* Tossup: 50-55%
* Tilt: 56-60%
* Lean: 61-75%
* Likely: 76-93%
* Safe: 94-100%

The ''New York Times''{{'}}s ''Upshot'' gave the Democrats a 60% chance of winning the Senate on August 24, 2016;<ref>{{cite news | last=Katz | first=Josh | date=August 24, 2016 | title=Democrats Have a 60 Percent Chance to Retake the Senate | url=https://www.nytimes.com/2016/08/24/upshot/democrats-have-a-60-percent-chance-to-retake-the-senate.html | newspaper=NYT | access-date=August 25, 2016}}</ref> on September 23, their model gave Republicans a 58% chance to maintain control.<ref name=":0">{{cite news | title=2016 Senate Election Forecast | newspaper=The New York Times | url=https://www.nytimes.com/interactive/2016/upshot/senate-election-forecast.html | date=September 23, 2016 | access-date=September 23, 2016| last1=Katz | first1=Josh }}</ref>

==Primary dates==
This table shows the primary dates for regularly scheduled elections. It also shows the [[Primary elections in the United States|type of primary]].
* "Open" primary: any registered voter can vote in any party's primary
* "Closed" primary, only voters registered with a specific party can vote in that party's primary.
* "[[Nonpartisan blanket primary|Top-two]]" primary, all candidates run against each other regardless of party affiliation, and the top two candidates advance to the second round of voting. (In Louisiana, a candidate can win the election by winning a majority of the vote in the first round.)
* All of the various other primary types are classified as "hybrid." Alaska in 2008 provides one example of a hybrid primary: The Democratic Party allowed unaffiliated voters to vote in its primary, while the Republican Party only allowed party members to vote in its primary.<ref name="primarytypes"/>

===Post-election changes===
One Republican resigned shortly after the start of the 115th Congress, and another Republican resigned during the 115th Congress for health reasons. Initially, all were replaced by Republican appointees. One Republican died during the 115th Congress and was replaced by a Republican appointee. One Democrat resigned during the 115th Congress and was replaced by a Democratic appointee. In Alabama, a [[2017 United States Senate special election in Alabama|2017 special election]] was held prior to the [[2020 United States Senate elections|2020 Senate elections]] for the remainder of the Class 2 term, where Democrat Doug Jones won the special election to succeed Republican appointee Luther Strange, who lost nomination to finish the term. In addition, one Republican died during the 115th Congress and was replaced by a former Senator who was appointed at that Congress.

== Alabama ==

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Alabama}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Alabama}}
Incumbent Republican [[Richard Shelby]] won re-election to a sixth term in office. The primaries were held on March 1. Ron Crumpton, a marijuana legalization activist, was the Democratic nominee.<ref name="Dean"/> Shelby won re-election with 63.9% of the vote.

Shelby was first elected to the Senate [[1986 United States Senate election in Alabama|in 1986]] as a Democrat and was easily re-elected [[1992 United States Senate election in Alabama|in 1992]] as such. He switched his party affiliation to Republican on November 9, 1994, one day after the Republicans won control of both houses in the [[1994 United States elections|midterm elections]]. He won his first full term as a Republican in [[1998 United States Senate election in Alabama|1998]] by a large margin and faced no significant opposition in [[2004 United States Senate election in Alabama|2004]] or [[2010 United States Senate election in Alabama|2010]].

If Shelby had decided to retire, numerous high-profile Alabama Republicans were speculated to run, including U.S. representatives [[Robert Aderholt]], [[Mo Brooks]], [[Bradley Byrne]], [[Gary Palmer (politician)|Gary Palmer]], [[Martha Roby]], and [[Mike Rogers (Alabama politician)|Mike Rogers]], State Treasurer [[Young Boozer]], State Speaker [[Mike Hubbard (politician)|Mike Hubbard]], Lieutenant Governor [[Kay Ivey]], State Senate President Pro Tempore [[Del Marsh]], Secretary of State [[John Merrill (American politician)|John Merrill]], U.S. Appeals Court Judge [[William H. Pryor Jr.]], former governor [[Bob Riley]], and Attorney General [[Luther Strange]].<ref name=FTAlabama>{{cite news | last1=Livingston | first1=Abby | title=Farm Team: Long Line Forming for GOP Hopefuls Looking to Move Up in Alabama | url=http://www.rollcall.com/news/farm_team_long_line_forming_for_gop_hopefuls_looking_to_move_up_in_alabama-222216-1.html | access-date=June 19, 2014 | publisher=Roll Call | date=February 6, 2013}}</ref><ref name=16in16>{{cite web | url=https://www.politico.com/story/2014/12/2016-senate-races-113837.html | title=16 in '16: The new battle for the Senate | date=December 29, 2014 | access-date=December 29, 2014 | work=[[Politico]] | last=Cheney | first=Kyle}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | title=Who is next? Five people who could be governor after the 2018 election | url=http://www.al.com/news/index.ssf/2014/09/five_people_who_could_be_gover.html | author=Jim Stinson | publisher=AL.com | date=September 18, 2014 | access-date=September 19, 2014}}</ref>  Shelby announced in January 2015 that he would run for re-election.<ref>{{cite web | url=http://www.al.com/opinion/index.ssf/2015/01/sen_richard_shelby_is_in_for_2.html | title=Sen. Richard Shelby is in for 2016 and that changes Alabama's political landscape | work=AL.com | last=Gore | first=Leada | date=January 29, 2015 | access-date=November 6, 2015}}</ref>

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Alaska}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Alaska}}
Two-term senator [[Lisa Murkowski]] (Republican) was appointed in 2002 and elected to a full term in 2004. She was defeated in the Republican primary in 2010 by [[Joe Miller (Alaska politician)|Joe Miller]]. She later ran as a write-in candidate in the 2010 general election and was re-elected to a second full term with 40% of the vote, making her one of two senators in US history to win election via write-in votes. She was 59 years old in 2016. She ran for re-election.<ref name=lisaannounces/>

Thomas Lamb, a candidate for the state House in [[2006 Alaska state elections|2006]], and Bob Lochner filed to run against Murkowski.<ref name=TLamb>{{cite web | title=August 16, 2016 Primary Candidate List | url=http://www.elections.alaska.gov/election/2016/Primary/candidate_info_prm_2016.php | publisher=State of Alaska Division of Elections | access-date=March 6, 2016 | archive-date=June 29, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160629003709/http://www.elections.alaska.gov/election/2016/Primary/candidate_info_prm_2016.php | url-status=dead }}</ref> Other potential Republican primary challengers included 2010 nominee and 2014 candidate [[Joe Miller (Alaska politician)|Joe Miller]], State Senator [[Mike J. Dunleavy]], former lieutenant governor [[Mead Treadwell]], and former mayor of [[Anchorage, Alaska|Anchorage]] [[Dan Sullivan (Mayor of Anchorage)|Dan Sullivan]].<ref>{{cite news | last=Pathe | first=Simone | date=May 15, 2015 | title=Murkowski Facing a Primary Puzzle | url=http://atr.rollcall.com/lisa-murkowski-primary-joe-miller-mike-dunleavy | work=[[Roll Call]] | access-date=December 23, 2015 | archive-date=December 15, 2015 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20151215093250/http://atr.rollcall.com/lisa-murkowski-primary-joe-miller-mike-dunleavy/ | url-status=dead }}</ref>

The only person to file for the Democratic primary as of May 20 was writer and satirist [[Richard Grayson (writer)|Richard Grayson]], who previously sought election to Wyoming's House seat in 2014.<ref>{{cite news | last1=Neary | first1=Ben | title=The Only Democrat Running For Congress In Wyoming Might Host A 'Campaign Rave' | 2016 | agency=Associated Press | date=June 13, 2013}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | last1=Lawrence | first1=Tom | title=EDITORIAL: Lessons we learned from Election Day | url=http://www.powelltribune.com/opinion/editorials/item/13047-editorial-lessons-we-learned-from-election-day | access-date=May 20, 2016 | publisher=Powell Tribune | date=November 6, 2014}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | last1=John | first1=Arit | title=Wyoming's Democratic 'Hip Hop Candidate' Just Launched the PPLZ 4 GRAYSON CREW | }}</ref><ref name=TLamb/> Potential Democratic candidates included State Senator [[Dennis Egan]], State Representative [[Andy Josephson]], State Senator [[Bill Wielechowski]], State Senator [[Hollis French]] and State Senate Minority Leader [[Johnny Ellis]].<ref name="Alaska Farm Team">{{cite news | last=Trygstad | first=Kyle | url=http://www.rollcall.com/news/farm_team_absence_of_opportunity_means_long_alaska_pipeline-222421-1.html?pg=2 | title=Farm Team: Absence of Opportunity Means Long Alaska Pipeline | senator [[Mark Begich]] was mentioned as a possible candidate,<ref>{{cite news | last=Sullivan | first=Sean| date=February 2, 2015 | newspaper=[[The Washington Post]] | url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/news/post-politics/wp/2015/02/02/why-mark-begich-could-run-for-the-senate-again | title=Why Mark Begich could run for the Senate again| access-date=February 16, 2015}}</ref> but he declined to run.<ref>{{cite news | url=http://www.adn.com/article/20160216/former-sen-begich-says-he-wont-run-any-office-year | last=Bohrer| first=Becky | date=February 16, 2016 | title=Begich says he won't run for any office this year | work=[[Alaska Dispatch News]]| access-date=February 18, 2016}}</ref>

Murkowski won her primary on August 16, 2016 with 72 percent of the vote. Joe Miller received the Libertarian nomination and ran against Murkowski in the general election. Anchorage attorney and veteran [[Margaret Stock]] ran as an Independent candidate.<ref name=Stock>{{cite news | last=Kelly | first=Devin | date=February 17, 2016 | title=Anchorage attorney Margaret Stock challenging Lisa Murkowski for Senate | url=http://www.adn.com/article/20160217/anchorage-attorney-margaret-stock-challenging-lisa-murkowski-senate | newspaper=Alaska Dispatch News | access-date=February 19, 2016}}</ref>

Murkowski won re-election with 44% of the vote, compared to Miller with 30% and Metcalfe with 11%. 15% went to other candidates. Murkowski has been re-elected three times now with 48% in 2004, 39.5% in 2010 and 44% in 2016, never having won a majority.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Arizona}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Arizona}}
Five-term senator and Republican presidential candidate in 2008 [[John McCain]] was re-elected with 59% of the vote in 2010. He was 80 years old in 2016. Despite speculation that he might retire,<ref name="McCain hint">{{cite news | last=Trujillo | first=Mario | title=McCain hints at retirement in 2016 | url=https://thehill.com/blogs/blog-briefing-room/news/182296-mccain-hints-at-retirement-in-2016/ | access-date=September 14, 2013 | newspaper=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] | date=September 13, 2013}}</ref> McCain ran for re-election.<ref name="movingparts"/>

McCain faced primary challenges from [[Fair Tax]] activist Alex Meluskey,{{Citation needed|date=October 2021}} businessman David Pizer,<ref name="Pizer">{{cite news | url=http://tucson.com/news/local/govt-and-politics/elections/pizer-known-for-upholstery-cryonics-is-latest-mccain-foe/article_5c84d37c-613f-54d9-8e17-807ef632ac40.html | title=Pizer, known for upholstery, cryonics, is latest McCain foe | work=Arizona Daily Star | date=November 9, 2015 | access-date=November 9, 2015}}</ref> talk radio host Clair Van Steenwyk,<ref name=VanSteenwyk>{{cite news | url=http://www.prescottenews.com/news/current-news/item/20079-letter-to-the-editor-why-im-running-for-us-senate | title=Letters to the Editor: Why I'm Running for US Senate | date=June 10, 2015 | access-date=December 5, 2015 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160906102032/http://www.prescottenews.com/news/current-news/item/20079-letter-to-the-editor-why-im-running-for-us-senate | archive-date=September 6, 2016 | url-status=dead | df=mdy-all }}</ref> and State Senator [[Kelli Ward]].<ref name="KelliWard">{{cite news | url=http://www.havasunews.com/news/kelli-ward-running-for-u-s-senate/article_188ea0b0-29cf-11e5-9953-d7c9d8bb6344.html | title=Havasu's Kelli Ward confirms Senate challenge against John McCain | work=Havasu News | date=July 14, 2015 | access-date=July 15, 2015}}</ref> David Pizer later dropped out of the race. Representatives [[Matt Salmon]] and [[David Schweikert]] were both mentioned as possible candidates,<ref>{{cite news | last1=Wong | first1=Scott | last2=Joseph | first2=Cameron | date=January 28, 2015 | url=https://thehill.com/homenews/house/230952-tea-party-lawmakers-we-may-take-on-mccain/ | title=Tea Party lawmakers may take on McCain | work=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]]}}</ref> but both chose not to run.<ref>{{cite news | last=Wong | first=Scott | date=January 29, 2015 | url=https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/255311-salmon-tells-mccain-i-wont-primary-you/ | title=Salmon tells McCain: I won't primary you | work=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]]}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | last=Nowicki | first=Dan | date=January 16, 2016 | work=[[The Arizona Republic]] | url=http://www.azcentral.com/story/news/arizona/politics/2016/01/16/maricopa-county-gop-slams-mccain-again-but-schweikert-wont-run/78914274 | title=Maricopa County GOP slams McCain again, but Schweikert won't run}}</ref> Other potential Republican candidates included former [[Governor of Arizona|governor]] [[Jan Brewer]],<ref>{{cite news | last=Mataconis | first=Doug | date=March 12, 2014 | url=http://www.outsidethebeltway.com/arizona-governor-jan-brewer-will-not-run-for-re-election | title=Arizona Governor Jan Brewer Will Not Run For Re-election | work=Outside the Beltway}}</ref> businesswoman and [[2014 Arizona gubernatorial election|2014]] gubernatorial candidate [[Christine Jones (businesswoman)|Christine Jones]],<ref>{{cite web | url=http://www.azcentral.com/story/azdc/2014/11/29/david-schweikert-christine-jones-john-mccain-2016-senate-primary/19562273 | title=Schweikert, Jones possible McCain primary foes in 2016? | publisher=AZ Central | date=November 29, 2014 | access-date=December 3, 2014}}</ref> former [[governor of Alaska]] and [[Vice presidential candidacy of Sarah Palin|2008 vice presidential candidate]] [[Sarah Palin]],<ref>{{cite news | url=https://www.nytimes.com/2014/11/26/us/obamas-immigration-action-reinvigorates-tea-party.html | title=After Obama's Immigration Action, a Blast of Energy for the Tea Party | newspaper=The New York Times | date=November 25, 2014 | access-date=December 3, 2014}}</ref> former U.S. representative [[John Shadegg]],<ref name="Last term">{{cite news | last=Nowicki | first=Dan | title=Is this Sen. McCain's last term? | url=http://www.azcentral.com/news/politics/articles/20130211mccain-is-this-last-term.html | access-date=September 21, 2013 | newspaper=The Arizona Republic | date=February 16, 2013 | archive-date=February 20, 2013 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20130220195149/http://www.azcentral.com/news/politics/articles/20130211mccain-is-this-last-term.html | url-status=dead }}</ref> and former Arizona attorney general [[Grant Woods (attorney)|Grant Woods]].<ref name="Last term"/>

Congresswoman [[Ann Kirkpatrick]]<ref name=kirkpatrickin/> and teacher Lennie Clark<ref name="dailykos.com">{{cite web | url=http://www.dailykos.com/story/2015/09/21/1423360/-Pro-Bernie-Candidate-for-Senate-in-Arizona | title=Pro-Bernie Candidate for Senate… in Arizona | author=millansingh | date=September 21, 2015}}</ref> ran for the Democratic nomination. Lennie Clark dropped out and Ann Kirkpatrick became the Democratic nominee. Other potential Democratic candidates included U.S. representative [[Ruben Gallego]], former [[Surgeon General of the United States|Surgeon General]] and 2012 nominee [[Richard Carmona]], 2014 gubernatorial nominee [[Fred DuVal]], Phoenix Mayor [[Greg Stanton]], and retired astronaut [[Mark Kelly]], the husband of ex-Congresswoman [[Gabby Giffords]].<ref name=16in16/><ref>{{cite web | url=http://www.azcentral.com/story/news/arizona/politics/2015/03/21/gosar-challenge-mccain/25170257 | title=Gosar won't challenge McCain | work=[[The Arizona Republic]] | last=Sanders | first=Rebekah L. | date=March 21, 2015 | access-date=March 24, 2015}}</ref>

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Arkansas}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Arkansas}}
One-term senator [[John Boozman]] (Republican) defeated two-term senator [[Blanche Lincoln]] with 58% of the vote in 2010. He was 65 years old in 2016. Despite speculation that he might retire following health problems,<ref>{{cite news | title=Arkansas US Sen. John Boozman Having Surgery | url=https://abcnews.go.com/US/wireStory/arkansas-us-sen-john-boozman-surgery-23423150 | access-date=April 22, 2014 | work=ABC News | date=April 22, 2014 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20140428010034/https://abcnews.go.com/US/wireStory/arkansas-us-sen-john-boozman-surgery-23423150 | archive-date=April 28, 2014 | url-status=dead | df=mdy-all }}</ref><ref>{{cite news | url=https://thehill.com/blogs/ballot-box/201883-senate-dems-see-uphill-fight-look-to-next-elections/ | title=Senate Democrats look to next elections | newspaper=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] | date=March 27, 2014 | access-date=October 25, 2014}}</ref> Boozman ran for re-election.<ref name="DeMillo"/> Fellow Republican Curtis Coleman, who ran against Boozman in 2010 but came in fifth place, ran again.<ref name="CurtisColeman">{{cite news | url=http://www.arktimes.com/ArkansasBlog/archives/2015/11/09/curtis-coleman-indicates-hell-take-on-incumbent-sen-john-boozman | title=Curtis Coleman indicates he'll take on incumbent Sen. John Boozman | work=Arkansas Times | date=November 9, 2015 | access-date=November 10, 2015}}</ref>

[[Conner Eldridge]], the former U.S. Attorney for the [[United States District Court for the Western District of Arkansas|Western District of Arkansas]], was the only Democrat who met the filing deadline.<ref>{{cite news | last1=Brantley | first1=Max | title=Conner Eldridge announces plans to run for U.S. Senate | url=http://www.arktimes.com/ArkansasBlog/archives/2015/09/09/conner-eldridge-announces-plans-to-run-for-us-senate | access-date=September 21, 2015 | newspaper=Arkansas Times | date=September 9, 2015}}</ref>

Frank Gilbert was the candidate for the Libertarian Party,<ref name=FGilbert>{{cite news | url=http://www.independentpoliticalreport.com/2015/10/arkansas-libertarians-nominate-twenty-three-candidates-including-frank-gilbert-for-u-s-senate | title=Arkansas Libertarians nominate twenty-three candidates, including Frank Gilbert for U.S. Senate | work=Independent Political Report | date=October 25, 2015 | access-date=November 10, 2015}}</ref><ref>{{cite web | publisher=Arkansas Secretary of State | url=https://candidates.arkansas.gov/?ac%3Ashow%3Acand_search=1&candid=201 | title=Candidate Information| access-date=February 4, 2016}}</ref><ref>{{cite web | url=http://lpar.org/elections/2016-candidates/2916-2 | title=Frank Gilbert, U.S. Senate | publisher=[[Libertarian Party of Arkansas]] | access-date=February 4, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160128134744/http://lpar.org/elections/2016-candidates/2916-2/ | archive-date=2016-01-28 | url-status=dead}}</ref> and Jason Tate was running a write-in campaign.<ref>{{cite web | url=http://www.sos.arkansas.gov/electionresults/index.php?ac:show:cand_search=1&candid=254 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20241227140936/https://www.sos.arkansas.gov/electionresults/index.php?ac:show:cand_search=1&candid=254 | url-status=dead | archive-date=December 27, 2024 | title=Candidate Information| publisher=Arkansas Secretary of State | access-date=February 4, 2016}}</ref>

Incumbent [[Republican Party (United States)|Republican]] Senator [[John Boozman]] won re-election to a second term in office, becoming the first Republican senator re-elected in the history of the state.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in California}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from California}}
Four-term senator [[Barbara Boxer]] (Democrat) was re-elected with 52% of the vote in 2010. Boxer declined to run for re-election.<ref name=boxerwontrun>{{cite web | url=https://www.politico.com/story/2015/01/barbara-boxer-retires-114076 | title=Barbara Boxer: Won't run again in 2016 | publisher=[[Politico]] | first=Burgess | last= Everett | date=January 8, 2015 | access-date=January 8, 2015}}</ref> [[California Attorney General]] [[Kamala Harris]] and U.S. representative [[Loretta Sanchez]], both Democrats, finished first and second, respectively,<ref name=CAPrimaryResults>{{cite web | url=http://vote.sos.ca.gov/returns/us-senate | title=U.S. Senate - Statewide Results | publisher=California Secretary of State | access-date=June 9, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20101105214455/http://vote.sos.ca.gov/returns/us-senate | archive-date=November 5, 2010 | url-status=dead | df=mdy-all }}</ref> in [[California Proposition 14 (2010)|California's nonpartisan blanket primary]], and contested the general election. As such, Boxer's successor was guaranteed to be a Democrat.<ref>{{cite news | last1=Nagourney | first1=Adam | first2= Jennifer | last2= Medina | url=https://www.nytimes.com/2016/06/09/us/just-how-bad-an-election-night-was-it-for-california-republicans.html | title=Just How Bad an Election Night Was It for California Republicans? | newspaper=The New York Times | date= June 8, 2016 | access-date= June 9, 2016}}</ref> This marked a historic first such occasion in California, ever since the Senate elections began in 1914.

Other Democrats on the primary ballot included "President" Cristina Grappo, Massie Munroe, Herbert Peters, Emory Rogers, and Steve Stokes.<ref name=CASoS>{{cite web | url=http://elections.cdn.sos.ca.gov/statewide-elections/2016-primary/june-2016-voter-nominated-candidates.pdf | title=Certified List of Candidates for Voter-Nominated Offices | date=April 1, 2016 | publisher=Secretary of State of California | access-date=April 5, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160418070732/http://elections.cdn.sos.ca.gov/statewide-elections/2016-primary/june-2016-voter-nominated-candidates.pdf | archive-date=2016-04-18 | url-status=dead }}</ref> Among the potential candidates who declined to run were [[Governor of California|Governor]] [[Jerry Brown]], [[Lieutenant Governor of California|Lieutenant Governor]] [[Gavin Newsom]], U.S. representatives [[Xavier Becerra]] and [[Adam Schiff]], and [[Mayor of Los Angeles|Los Angeles Mayor]] [[Eric Garcetti]].

Former state Republican Party chairs Tom Del Beccaro<ref name="Beccaro">{{cite news | url=https://www.sacbee.com/news/politics-government/capitol-alert/article19436328.html | title=Republican Tom Del Beccaro announces U.S. Senate run | first=Christopher | last=Cadelago | date=April 26, 2015 | work=The Sacramento Bee}}</ref> and Duf Sundheim,<ref name="Sundheim">{{cite news | url=http://www.latimes.com/local/political/la-me-pc-sundheim-senate-campaign-boxer-20150520-story.html | title=Duf Sundheim enters race for Barbara Boxer's Senate seat | work=Los Angeles Times | date=September 9, 2015}}</ref> and former state senator [[Phil Wyman]]<ref name="PWyman">{{cite news | last=Hrabe | first=John | url=http://www.calnewsroom.com/2015/01/25/us-senate-2016-phil-wyman-joins-list-of-gop-longshot-candidates | title=US Senate 2016: Wyman joins list of GOP longshot candidates | date=January 25, 2015 | work=CalNewsroom.com| access-date=March 4, 2016}}</ref><ref>{{cite web | url=http://www.philwymanforsenate.org | title= Phil Wyman for United States Senate | access-date=March 4, 2016}}</ref> ran, along with Don Krampe,<ref>{{cite news | first=Jeff| last=Horseman | url=https://www.pressenterprise.com/2015/01/21/us-senate-don-krampe-of-murrieta-to-seek-barbara-boxer8217s-seat/ | title=U.S. SENATE: Done Krampe of Murrieta to seek Barbara Boxer's seat | date=January 21, 2015 | work=The Press Enterprise}}</ref> Tom Palzer,<ref>{{cite news | last=Provost | first=Lee | url=http://www.daily-journal.com/news/local/palzer-former-kankakeean-makes-run-for-us-senate-in-his/article_11b6abea-8ad8-50c2-844e-59eabad5f5c4.html| title=Palzer, former Kankakeean, makes run for US Senate in his new home of Calif. | date=April 14, 2015}}</ref> Karen Roseberry,<ref>{{cite web | last=Roseberry | first=Karen | date=October 28, 2015 | url=http://karenroseberryforsenate.com | title=Karen Roseberry for U.S. Senate 2016}}</ref> Greg Conlon, Von Huogo, Jerry Laws, Ron Unz, Jarrell Williamson, and George Yang.<ref name=CASoS/> State Assemblymen [[Rocky Chavez]] was running as well,<ref>{{cite news | last=Cadelago | first=Christopher | date=March 5, 2015 | access-date=March 15, 2015 | url=https://www.sacbee.com/news/politics-government/capitol-alert/article12602606.html | title=Republican Rocky Chavez launches bid for U.S. Senate seat | work=Sacramento Bee}}</ref> but withdrew from the race.<ref>{{cite news | last=Cadelago | first=Christopher | url=https://www.sacbee.com/news/politics-government/capitol-alert/article59229823.html | title=Chaves withdraws from U.S. Senate race, will seek re-election to Assembly | date=February 8, 2016 | work=Sacramento Bee | access-date=February 11, 2016}}</ref> Republicans who were once considered potential candidates but ruled out runs included [[Mayor of San Diego|San Diego Mayor]] [[Kevin Faulconer]], former [[Assistant Secretary of the Treasury for Financial Stability]] and [[2014 California gubernatorial election|2014 gubernatorial nominee]] [[Neel Kashkari]], U.S. representative [[Darrell Issa]], and businesswoman and nominee for the U.S. Senate [[2010 United States Senate election in California|in 2010]] [[Carly Fiorina]].<ref>{{cite web | last=Marinucci | first=Carla | url=http://www.sfgate.com/politics/article/Carly-Fiorina-won-t-rule-out-run-for-White-House-5775910.php | title=Carly Fiorina won't rule out run for White House | date=September 24, 2014 | access-date=September 26, 2014 | work=SF Gate}}</ref>

Independent Mike Beitiks ran on a single-issue [[climate change]] platform.<ref>{{cite news | last1=Smith | first1=Heather | title=California Senate candidate: "We're all going to die" | url=http://grist.org/politics/california-senate-candidate-were-all-going-to-die | access-date=June 4, 2015 | publisher=Grist | date=May 29, 2015}}</ref>

Polling conducted by the SurveyUSA from March 30, 2016 to April 3, 2016 indicated that Harris was ahead with 26%, compared to Rep. Sánchez with 22%, Del Beccaro with 8%, Wyman with 8%, and Sundheim with 3%; 7% of those polled were supporting other candidates, and 24% were undecided.<ref>{{cite news | date=April 4, 2016 | url=http://www.surveyusa.com/client/PollReport.aspx?g=4114a2c6-213f-4447-8c7b-eb137d8c3312 | title=Results of SurveyUSA Election Poll #22817 | work=SurveyUSA | access-date=April 5, 2016}}</ref>

Harris won the election with 62% of the vote to Sanchez's 38%.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Colorado}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Colorado}}
One-term senator [[Michael Bennet]] (Democrat) was appointed in 2009 and elected to a full term with 48% of the vote in 2010. He was 51 years old in 2016. He ran for re-election.<ref name=glennearlyann/>

Businessman Robert Blaha,<ref name=Blaha>{{cite news | url=http://blogs.denverpost.com/thespot/2016/01/14/robert-blaha-announces-us-senate-bid-makes-campaign-pledge/124487 | title=Robert Blaha announces U.S. Senate bid, makes campaign pledge | last=Frank | first=John | date=January 14, 2016 | work=[[The Denver Post]] | access-date=January 21, 2016 | archive-date=January 17, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160117134021/http://blogs.denverpost.com/thespot/2016/01/14/robert-blaha-announces-us-senate-bid-makes-campaign-pledge/124487/ | url-status=dead }}</ref> former [[Aurora, Colorado|Aurora]] councilman Ryan Frazier,<ref name="RyanFrazier">{{cite news | url=http://www.aurorasentinel.com/news/former-aurora-councilman-ryan-frazier-joins-gop-senate-race-in-colorado | title=Former Aurora councilman Ryan Frazier joins GOP Senate race in Colorado | work=Aurora Sentinel | date=November 12, 2015 | access-date=November 13, 2015}}</ref> [[El Paso County, Colorado|El Paso County]] [[County commission|Commissioners]] Darryl Glenn,<ref name="DarrylGlenn"/> and [[Peggy Littleton]],<ref name="PLittleton">{{cite news | last=Matthews | first=Mark | url=http://www.denverpost.com/election/ci_29407378/el-paso-countys-peg-littleton-joins-u-s | title=El Paso County's Peg Littleton joins U.S. Senate race | date=January 20, 2016 | work=[[The Denver Post]] | access-date=January 21, 2016}}</ref> former [[Colorado State University]] Athletic Director Jack Graham,<ref name=JackGraham>{{cite news | last=Lyell | first=Kelly | url=http://www.coloradoan.com/story/news/politics/elections/2016/01/29/former-csu-ad-jack-graham-enters-senate-race/79526786 | title=Former CSU AD Jack Graham enters U.S. Senate race | work=[[Fort Collins Coloradoan]] | date=January 29, 2016 | access-date=February 1, 2016}}</ref> [[Colorado House of Representatives|State Representative]] [[Jon Keyser]],<ref name="JKeyser">{{cite news | url=http://www.denverpost.com/election/ci_29370305/jon-keyser-announces-2016-senate-bid-amid-crowded | title=Jon Keyser announces 2016 Senate bid amid crowded Colorado field | work=[[The Denver Post]] | date=January 11, 2016 | access-date=January 11, 2016}}</ref> former [[Small Business Administration|SBA]] director Greg Lopez,<ref name="GregLopez">{{cite news | last=Bartels | first=Lynn | url=http://blogs.denverpost.com/thespot/2015/07/06/greg-lopez-former-sba-director-u-s-senate/121794 | title=Republican Greg Lopez, former SBA director, to run for U.S. Senate | work=[[The Denver Post]] | date=July 6, 2015 | access-date=October 14, 2015 | archive-date=September 8, 2015 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150908071507/http://blogs.denverpost.com/thespot/2015/07/06/greg-lopez-former-sba-director-u-s-senate/121794/ | url-status=dead }}</ref> [[Colorado Senate|State Senator]] [[Tim Neville]],<ref name="Neville">{{cite news | last=Frank| first=John | url=http://www.denverpost.com/election/ci_28891304/tim-neville-announce-u-s-senate-bid-gop | title=Tim Neville to announce U.S. Senate bid as GOP race heats up | work=[[The Denver Post]] | date=September 28, 2015 | access-date=September 29, 2015}}</ref> and [[Jefferson County, Colorado|Jefferson County]] [[County Commission|Commissioner]] Donald Rosier<ref name=Rosier>{{cite news | last=Bunch | first=Joey | url=http://www.denverpost.com/election/ci_29225364/jefferson-county-commissioner-join-race-u-s-senate | title=Jefferson County commissioner to join race for U.S. Senate Thursday | work=[[The Denver Post]] | date=December 9, 2015 | access-date=December 17, 2015}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | url=https://www.facebook.com/donrosier/photos/a.10152977145945063.1073741829.485233530062/10156208240340063/?type=3&theater | title= Donald Rosier - Timeline Photos | date=December 7, 2015}}</ref> ran for the Republican nomination. Glenn, Graham, Blaha, Keyser, and Frazier actually competed in the primary.<ref name=DarrylGlennWinsPrimary/>

Darryl Glenn won the Republican nomination with 37% of the vote against four other opponents.<ref name=DarrylGlennWinsPrimary>{{cite news | url=https://www.denverpost.com/2016/06/28/colorado-senate-primary | title=Darryl Glenn takes Colorado Republican nomination for U.S. Senate | last=Frank | first=John | work= [[The Denver Post]] | date=June 28, 2016 | access-date=June 29, 2016}}</ref>

Bennet won re-election with 50% of the vote to Glenn's 44%.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Connecticut}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Connecticut}}
One-term senator [[Richard Blumenthal]] (Democrat) was elected with 55% of the vote in 2010. He was 70 years old in 2016. He ran for re-election.<ref name=RCRG/>

State Representative [[Dan Carter (American politician)|Dan Carter]],<ref name=DCarter/> apparel company CEO and [[2004 United States Senate election in Connecticut|2004]] Senate nominee [[Jack Orchulli]],<ref name=Orchulli>{{cite news | title=Darien fashion label founder Orchulli to run for Senate | url=http://www.ctpost.com/local/article/Darien-fashion-label-founder-Orchulli-to-run-for-7244196.php | last=Vigdor | first=Neil| date=April 12, 2016 | work=The Connecticut Post | access-date=April 16, 2016}}</ref> and former Olympic athlete [[August Wolf]]<ref name=AWolf>{{cite news | last=Radelat | first=Ana | url=http://ctmirror.org/2015/05/04/blumenthal-draws-gop-challenger | title=Stamford Republican steps up to challenge Blumenthal| work=the CT Mirror | date=May 4, 2015 | access-date=May 15, 2015}}</ref> ran for the Republican nomination. Another potential candidate was former [[West Hartford, Connecticut|West Hartford]] Town Councilor Joe Visconti, who ran for [[Connecticut's 1st congressional district|CT-01]] in [[2008 United States House of Representatives elections in Connecticut|2008]] and ran as an independent for governor in [[2014 Connecticut gubernatorial election|2014]].<ref>{{cite news | last=Vigdor | first=Neil | date=May 1, 2015 | work=[[Connecticut Post]] | url=http://blog.ctnews.com/politics/2015/05/01/visconti-exploring-2016-senate-run | title=Visconti exploring 2016 Senate run}}</ref> Former [[Comptroller General of the United States|U.S. Comptroller General]] and 2014 candidate for [[Lieutenant Governor of Connecticut|Lieutenant Governor]] [[David M. Walker (politician)|David M. Walker]],<ref name=demseats>{{cite web | url=http://usconservatives.about.com/od/campaignselections/fl/Democratic-Seats-up-for-Re-Election-in-2016.htm | title=Democratic Seats up for Re-Election in 2016 | date=September 30, 2014 | access-date=October 3, 2014 | last=Hawkins | first=Dustin | work=[[About.com|About News]] | archive-date=October 6, 2014 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20141006065318/http://usconservatives.about.com/od/campaignselections/fl/Democratic-Seats-up-for-Re-Election-in-2016.htm | url-status=dead }}</ref><ref>{{cite news | url=http://www.ctpost.com/local/article/Walker-won-t-challenge-Blumenthal-for-Senate-6153442.php | title=Walker won't challenge Blumenthal for Senate | newspaper=[[Connecticut Post]] | last=Vigdor | first=Neil | date=March 23, 2015| access-date=March 25, 2015}}</ref> former U.S. representative and 2010 candidate [[Rob Simmons]],<ref>{{cite news | last=Vigdor | first=Neil | url=http://blog.ctnews.com/politics/2015/04/08/simmons-rules-out-2016-challenge-of-blumenthal | title=Simmons rules out 2016 challenge of Blumenthal | newspaper=[[Connecticut Post]] | date=April 8, 2015 | access-date=April 9, 2015}}</ref> and economist and former [[CNBC]] television host [[Lawrence Kudlow]] declined to run.<ref>{{cite news | last=Everett | first=Burgess | date=January 14, 2016 | url=https://www.politico.com/story/2016/01/larry-kudlow-interested-senate-run-217765 | title=Kudlow said to be 'very interested' in Senate run | work=[[Politico]] | access-date=January 26, 2016}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | last=Byers | first=Dylan | date=February 16, 2016 | work=[[CNNMoney]] | url=https://money.cnn.com/2016/02/16/media/larry-kudlow-senate-cnbc/index.html | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160226192415/http://money.cnn.com/2016/02/16/media/larry-kudlow-senate-cnbc/index.html | url-status=dead | archive-date=February 26, 2016 | title=CNBC's Larry Kudlow will not run for Senate| access-date=February 18, 2016}}</ref>

Blumenthal won re-election with 63% of the vote to Carter's 35%.

== Florida ==

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Florida}}
One-term senator [[Marco Rubio]] (Republican) was elected in a three-way race with 49% of the vote in 2010. In April 2014, Rubio stated that he would not run for both the Senate and President in 2016, as Florida law prohibits a candidate from appearing twice on a ballot.<ref name=Berman>{{cite magazine | last=Berman | first=Matt | title=Marco Rubio Won't Run for Senate in 2016 if He Runs for President | url=http://www.nationaljournal.com/politics/marco-rubio-won-t-run-for-senate-in-2016-if-he-runs-for-president-20140402 | date=April 2, 2014 | access-date=April 2, 2014 | magazine=National Journal}}</ref> In April 2015, he announced that he would run for [[Marco Rubio 2016 presidential campaign|President]] and would not seek re-election.<ref name=rubiotells>{{cite news | url=http://www.cbsnews.com/news/marco-rubio-tells-donors-hes-running-for-president-in-2016 | title=Marco Rubio tells donors he's running for president in 2016 | work=CBS News | date=April 13, 2015 | access-date=April 13, 2015}}</ref> After suspending his campaign on March 15, 2016, Rubio announced on June 22, 2016 that he had changed his mind and would run for re-election.<ref name=Rubio/>

U.S. representative [[Ron DeSantis]], combat veteran Todd Wilcox,<ref>{{cite web | url=http://www.saintpetersblog.com/archives/235021 | title=Entrepreneur and combat veteran Todd Wilcox enters Florida's U.S. Senate race | first= Mitch | last=Perry | date=July 8, 2015| publisher=Saint Peters Blog}}</ref> real estate developer Carlos Beruff,<ref>{{cite news | last=Wallace | first=Jeremy | date=February 24, 2016 | work=[[Miami Herald]] | url=http://www.miamiherald.com/news/politics-government/state-politics/article62332362.html | title=Cuban-American developer Carlos Beruff launching campaign for U.S. Senate in Miami | access-date=February 26, 2016}}</ref> retired college lecturer Ilya Katz,<ref name=Katz>{{cite web | access-date=March 1, 2016 | url=http://www.katz4uscongress.com | title=Katz for US Congress | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160304101450/http://www.katz4uscongress.com/ | archive-date=2016-03-04 | url-status=dead }}</ref> and Donald J. DeRenzo ran for the Republican nomination.<ref name=USElections>{{cite web | url=http://www.uselections.com/fl/fl.htm | title=US Elections -- Florida | access-date=March 1, 2016}}</ref><ref name="auto2">{{cite web | url=http://florida.state-election.info | title=2016 Florida Elections | access-date=March 1, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160302122811/http://florida.state-election.info/ | archive-date=2016-03-02 | url-status=dead }}</ref> [[Ben Carson]], retired neurosurgeon and candidate for [[President of the United States|president]] in [[Ben Carson presidential campaign, 2016|2016]], was also mentioned as a potential candidate.<ref>{{cite web | url=https://edition.cnn.com/2016/03/01/politics/ben-carson-answered-prayers/index.html | title=GOP operatives to pitch Ben Carson on Florida Senate run | work=[[CNN]] | last=Bash | first=Dana | date=March 2, 2016 | access-date=March 2, 2016}}</ref> On June 17, 2016, U.S. representative [[David Jolly]] withdrew from the race to run for re-election to his House seat, four days after Rubio began openly considering reversing his decision to not run for re-election.<ref>{{cite news | last=Kopan | first=Tal | url=http://www.cnn.com/2016/06/17/politics/david-jolly-marco-rubio-senate-race | title=David Jolly drops out of Florida Senate race, possibly clearing way for Marco Rubio | work=[[CNN]] | date=June 17, 2016 | access-date=June 18, 2016}}</ref>

U.S. representative [[Patrick Murphy (Florida politician)|Patrick Murphy]]<ref name="Marc Caputo"/> defeated fellow representative [[Alan Grayson]], as well as Pam Keith, Lateresa Jones, Richard Coleman, Sam Brian Gibbons, and Josh Larose, for the Democratic nomination. Murphy lost to incumbent [[Marco Rubio]] in the November general election on November 8.<ref>{{cite web | url=http://www.news4jax.com/news/elections/rubio-defeats-murphy-to-retain-senate-seat | title=Rubio defeats Murphy to retain Senate seat | date=November 9, 2016 }}</ref>

Sen. Rubio won re-election with 52% of the vote, compared to Murphy's 44%.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Georgia}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Georgia}}
Two-term senator [[Johnny Isakson]] (Republican) was re-elected with 58% of the vote in 2010. He was 71 years old in 2016. He ran for re-election.<ref name=isaksonthird/> In 2015, Isakson announced he was being treated for [[Parkinson's disease]], but stated that his treatment would not interfere with his re-election campaign or his ability to serve another term.<ref name=arappeport>{{cite news | last1=Rappeport | first1=Alan | title=Johnny Isakson, Georgia Senator, Says He Has Parkinson's | url=https://www.nytimes.com/politics/first-draft/2015/06/10/johnny-isakson-georgia-senator-says-he-has-parkinsons | access-date=June 12, 2015 | newspaper=The New York Times | date=June 10, 2015}}</ref>

Mary Kay Bacallao, college professor, former [[Fayette County School System (Georgia)|Fayette County Board of Education]] member, and candidate for [[Georgia Department of Education|State Superintendent of Schools]] in [[2014 Georgia elections|2014]]<ref name=Bacallao>{{cite web | last=Foody | first=Kathleen | url=http://www.wsbtv.com/news/georgia/qualifying-for-georgias-may-primaries-ends/156636356 | title=Qualifying for Georgia's May primaries ends | date=March 11, 2016 | work=WSB TV | access-date=April 19, 2016 | archive-date=March 12, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160312033042/http://www.wsbtv.com/news/georgia/qualifying-for-georgias-may-primaries-ends/156636356 | url-status=dead }}</ref> and Derrick Grayson, candidate for the state's other Senate seat in 2014,<ref name=DGrayson>{{cite news | last=Malloy | first=Daniel | url=http://politics.blog.ajc.com/2015/01/09/it-looks-like-johnny-isakson-has-his-first-challenger | title=It looks like Johnny Isakson has his first challenger | work=[[The Atlanta Journal-Constitution]] | date=January 9, 2015 | access-date=May 15, 2015 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150117003607/http://politics.blog.ajc.com/2015/01/09/it-looks-like-johnny-isakson-has-his-first-challenger | archive-date=January 17, 2015 | url-status=dead | df=mdy-all }}</ref> challenged Isakson for the Republican nomination. Isakson won the Republican nomination with more than three quarters of the vote.<ref name="AJC results">{{cite news | url=http://politics.blog.ajc.com/2016/05/24/johnny-isakson-trounces-rivals-to-win-georgia-gop-senate-nod | last=Bluestein | first=Greg | title=Johnny Isakson trounces rivals to win Georgia GOP Senate nod | publisher=The Atlanta Journal-Constitution | date=May 24, 2016 | access-date=June 16, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160623180419/http://politics.blog.ajc.com/2016/05/24/johnny-isakson-trounces-rivals-to-win-georgia-gop-senate-nod | archive-date=June 23, 2016 | url-status=dead | df=mdy-all }}</ref>

Investment firm executive Jim Barksdale,<ref name=Barksdale/> project manager Cheryl Copeland,<ref name=Copeland>{{cite news | date=March 8, 2016 | work=[[The Albany Herald]] | url=http://www.albanyherald.com/news/local/government/southwest-georgia-s-congressional-delegation-qualifies-for-re-election/article_f2d69688-e9c3-5ab0-b2e7-f92794ba3f5c.html | title=Southwest Georgia's congressional delegation qualifies for re-election| access-date=March 10, 2016}}</ref> and businessman John Coyne<ref name=Coyne>{{cite news | last=Bluestein | first=Greg | url=http://politics.blog.ajc.com/2016/03/06/more-democrats-come-out-of-woodwork-in-georgia-senate-race | title=More Democrats come out of woodwork for Georgia Senate race | date=March 6, 2016 | work=[[The Atlanta Journal-Constitution]] | access-date=April 19, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160307121825/http://politics.blog.ajc.com/2016/03/06/more-democrats-come-out-of-woodwork-in-georgia-senate-race | archive-date=March 7, 2016 | url-status=dead | df=mdy-all }}</ref> ran for the Democratic nomination. USAF veteran Jim Knox was running but dropped out of the race.<ref>{{cite news | last=Hankerson | first=Jazmyne | date=April 8, 2016 | access-date=April 20, 2016 | url=http://wfxl.com/news/local/candidate-changes-affects-may-primary-ballot | title=Candidate changes affects May primary ballot | work=[[WXFL]]}}</ref> Barksdale defeated Copeland in a close race to win the Democratic nomination.<ref name="AJC results"/>

Sen. Isakson won re-election with 55% to Barksdale's 41%.

| before_party       = Democratic Party (United States)
| after_election     = [[Brian Schatz]]
| after_party        = Democratic Party (United States)
}}
{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Hawaii}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Hawaii}}
In 2012, Hawaii governor [[Neil Abercrombie]] appointed Lieutenant Governor [[Brian Schatz]] (Democrat) to take the place of deceased nine-term senator [[Daniel Inouye]]. Schatz won a [[2014 United States Senate special election in Hawaii|2014 special election]] to serve the remainder of Inouye's term. Schatz ran for re-election.<ref name=RCRG/>

Former U.S. representative and [[2014 United States Senate special election in Hawaii|2014 Senate candidate]] [[Colleen Hanabusa]] considered challenging Schatz in the primary again,<ref>{{cite web |title = Dan Inouye and Brian Schatz: A Lesson in Hawaii Politics | url = http://www.civilbeat.com/2014/08/dan-inouye-and-brian-schatz-a-lesson-in-hawaii-politics | publisher = Honolulu Civil Beat | date = August 18, 2014 | author = Stephen O'Harrow}}</ref> while U.S. representative [[Tulsi Gabbard]] declined to seek the Democratic nomination for the seat.<ref>{{cite web | url=http://www.civilbeat.com/2015/03/tulsi-gabbard-looks-to-be-running-for-re-election | title=Tulsi Gabbard Looks to Be Running for Re-election | publisher=Honolulu Civil Beat | date=March 14, 2015 | first=Chad | last= Blair}}</ref>

Charles Collins, a Republican who ran for the Senate in [[2012 United States Senate election in Hawaii|2012]] and for governor in [[2014 Hawaii gubernatorial election|2014]], was seeking the nomination again,<ref>{{cite web | url=http://charlescollinsussenatehi.yolasite.com | title=Charles Collins for US Senate | date=January 22, 2016}}</ref> but withdrew from the race.<ref>{{cite news | url=https://ballotpedia.org/Charles_Collins |website=Ballotpedia |title=Charles Collins | date=January 25, 2016}}</ref>

Sen. Schatz won re-election with 74% of the vote, compared to Carroll's 22%.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Idaho}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Idaho}}
Three-term senator [[Mike Crapo]] (Republican) was re-elected with 71% of the vote in 2010. Crapo was 65 years old in 2016. He ran for re-election.<ref name="kboi"/> U.S Representative [[Raul Labrador]] declined to challenge Crapo in the Republican primary.<ref>{{cite news | title=Crapo's Message To Labrador | url=http://www.spokesman.com/blogs/hbo/2014/aug/20/crapos-message-labrador | access-date=September 3, 2014 | newspaper=[[The Spokesman-Review]] | date=August 20, 2014}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | url=https://thehill.com/blogs/ballot-box/senate-races/232772-labrador-i-dont-intend-to-primary-crapo/ | title=Labrador won't run for Senate | work=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] | last=Easley | first=Jonathan | date=February 13, 2015 | access-date=February 16, 2015}}</ref>

Jerry Sturgill ran for the Democratic nomination.<ref name=Sturgill/>

Perennial candidate [[Pro-Life (politician)|Pro-Life]] ran as an independent.<ref name=ProLife>{{cite web | last=Brown | first=Nathan | url=http://magicvalley.com/news/local/govt-and-politics/federal-lands-refugees-and-regulations-drive-crapo-town-hall-in/article_15cf766d-e0a3-5374-9f55-e4d122e2fe98.html | title=Federal Land, Refugees, and Regulations Drive Crapo Town Hall in Castleford | date=August 12, 2015 | work=[[Times-News (Idaho)|Twin Falls Times-News]] | access-date=March 4, 2016}}</ref><ref>{{cite web | url=http://prolifeidaho.com | title=Pro-Life For Governor of Idaho – 2014, also US Senate – 2016 | access-date=March 4, 2016 | url-status=dead | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160302115302/http://prolifeidaho.com/ | archive-date=March 2, 2016 | df=mdy-all }}</ref> He was defeated in the [[Constitution Party (United States)|Constitution Party]] primary on May 17, 2016 to Ray J. Writz.<ref name=IDOfficialresults/>

Sen. Crapo was re-elected.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Illinois}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Illinois}}
One-term senator [[Mark Kirk]] (Republican) was elected with 48% of the vote in 2010. He was 57 years old in 2016. He ran for re-election. Kirk suffered a stroke in January 2012 that kept him away from the Senate until January 2013.<ref>{{cite news | first=Ed | last= O'Keefe | title=Mark Kirk makes dramatic return to the Senate | url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/blogs/post-politics/wp/2013/01/03/mark-kirk-makes-dramatic-return-to-the-senate | access-date=July 17, 2014 | newspaper=The Washington Post | date=January 3, 2013}}</ref> In June 2013, he confirmed that he was planning to run for re-election,<ref>{{cite magazine | first=Matthew | last=Cooper | title=Mark Kirk Survived a Stroke – Now He's Picking Fights in Congress | url=http://www.nationaljournal.com/politics/mark-kirk-survived-a-stroke-now-he-s-picking-fights-in-congress-20130628 | access-date=July 17, 2014 | magazine=National Journal | date=June 28, 2013 | url-status=dead | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20130701014650/http://www.nationaljournal.com/politics/mark-kirk-survived-a-stroke-now-he-s-picking-fights-in-congress-20130628 | archive-date=July 1, 2013 }}</ref> but speculation persisted that he might retire.<ref name=farmteamil>{{cite news | last=Cahn | first=Emily | title=Illinois Options Depend on Chicago Turnover Farm Team | url=http://www.rollcall.com/news/illinois_options_depend_on_chicago_turnover_farm_team-225575-1.html?pg=2 | access-date=August 14, 2013 | publisher=[[Roll Call]] | date=June 12, 2013}}</ref> In November 2014, Kirk reiterated that he was going to run for re-election, saying: "No frickin' way am I retiring."<ref name=frickin>{{cite web | url=http://atr.rollcall.com/elections-2016-mark-kirk-illinois-senate-race | title=Mark Kirk: 'No Frickin' Way Am I Retiring' | publisher=[[Roll Call]] | last=Cahn | first=Emily | date=November 14, 2014 | access-date=January 1, 2015 | archive-date=December 29, 2014 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20141229192712/http://atr.rollcall.com/elections-2016-mark-kirk-illinois-senate-race/ | url-status=dead }}</ref>

[[Joe Walsh (Illinois politician)|Joe Walsh]], a former U.S. representative and conservative talk radio host, declined to challenge Kirk in the Republican primary.<ref>{{cite news | url=http://www.dailyherald.com/article/20151130/news/151139890 | author=Mike Riopell | title=Who's in, who's out in congressional primaries | date=November 30, 2015 | access-date=December 1, 2015 | work=Daily Herald}}</ref> Two others filed for the right to challenge Senator Kirk in the primary: businessman James Marter,<ref>{{cite web | author=Greg Hinz | url=http://www.chicagobusiness.com/article/20151005/BLOGS02/151009924/kirk-picks-up-primary-challenger-from-the-right | title=Kirk picks up primary challenger from the right | date=October 5, 2015 | work=[[Crain's Chicago Business]]}}</ref> and Elizabeth Pahlke,<ref>{{cite news | author=Rick Pearson | author2=Hal Dardick | date=December 1, 2015 | access-date=December 1, 2015 | title=U.S. Senate, state's attorney races take shape on the last day of filing | url=http://www.chicagotribune.com/news/local/politics/ct-illinois-primary-march-2016-candidate-filing-met-1201-20151130-story.html | work=[[Chicago Tribune]]}}</ref> but Pahlke was disqualified, so only Marter was on the ballot running against Kirk.<ref>{{cite news | url=http://fox2now.com/2016/01/08/1-of-sen-kirks-gop-primary-opponents-doesnt-make-ballot | title=1 of Sen. Kirk's GOP primary opponents doesn't make ballot | date=January 8, 2016 | access-date=January 17, 2016 | work=[[KTVI]]}}</ref> On March 15, Kirk won the primary with 71% of the vote.<ref name=WBEZ>{{cite news | date=March 15, 2016 | work=WBEZ 91.5 Chicago | url=https://www.wbez.org/shows/-/-/e50cb21d-26b1-47ef-9097-9357121c9061 | title=2016 Illinois Primary Election Results | access-date=March 18, 2016}}</ref>

U.S. representative [[Tammy Duckworth]],<ref>{{cite news | last=Skiba | first=Katherine | date=March 30, 2015 | work=[[Chicago Tribune]] | url=http://www.chicagotribune.com/news/local/politics/ct-tammy-duckworth-senate-mark-kirk-20150330-story.html#page=1 | title=Duckworth to Challenge Kirk for U.S. Senate | access-date=March 30, 2015}}</ref> President and CEO of the Chicago Urban League, Andrea Zopp,<ref>{{cite news | last=Sneed | first=Michael | url=http://chicago.suntimes.com/news/7/71/607839/sneed-zopp-senate-race | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150517232706/http://chicago.suntimes.com/news/7/71/607839/sneed-zopp-senate-race | url-status=dead | archive-date=May 17, 2015 | title=Andrea Zopp is all in for Senate race | date=May 14, 2015}}</ref> and State Senator [[Napoleon Harris]] ran for the Democratic nomination.<ref>{{cite news | last=Hinz | first=Greg | url=http://www.chicagobusiness.com/article/20151027/BLOGS02/151029840/state-sen-napoleon-harris-to-enter-race-against-mark-kirk | title=State Sen. Napoleon Harris to enter race against Mark Kirk | date=October 27, 2015}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | url=http://www.uselections.com/il/il.htm | title=2016 Illinois Election, Primary, Candidates, Races, and Voting | work=USElections| date=December 2, 2015 | access-date=December 3, 2015}}</ref> On March 15, Duckworth won the primary with 64% of the vote.<ref name=WBEZ/>

In December 2015, Jim Brown, a teacher and former businessman, announced he was running as an independent.<ref>{{cite news | url=http://jimbrownforussenate.com | title=Jim Brown Independent for U.S. Senate | access-date=December 3, 2015 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20151208124014/http://jimbrownforussenate.com/ | archive-date=2015-12-08 | url-status=dead }}</ref>

Chris Aguayo, an Iraq/Afghan War veteran and Veterans Party State Chair, announced he was running, representing the Veterans Party.<ref>{{cite web | url=http://www.chrisaguayo2016.com | title=Chris Aguayo for United States Senate | access-date=June 15, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160509025030/http://www.chrisaguayo2016.com/ | archive-date=May 9, 2016 | url-status=dead | df=mdy-all }}</ref>

Rep. Duckworth unseated Sen. Kirk with 55% compared to his 40%.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Indiana}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Indiana}}
Three-term senator [[Dan Coats]] (Republican) was elected with 55% of the vote in 2010; Coats served in the Senate from 1989 to 1999 and then returned to serve another term from 2011 to 2017. Coats did not run for re-election.<ref name=Cheney2>{{cite news | last1=Cheney | first1=Kyle | title=Democrats see glimmer of hope in Dan Coats' retirement | url=https://www.politico.com/story/2015/03/dan-coats-retirement-democrats-hopeful-116358 | access-date=March 28, 2015 | publisher=[[Politico]] | date=March 24, 2015}}</ref> Republican candidates included U.S. representatives [[Marlin Stutzman]]<ref name=Stutzman>{{cite news | date=May 13, 2015 | url=http://www.realclearpolitics.com/2015/05/13/rep_marlin_stutzman_declares_senate_bid_356965.html | title=Rep. Marlin Stutzman declares Senate bid | work=Real Clear Politics | access-date=May 16, 2015}}</ref> and [[Todd Young]].<ref name=ToddYoung/> Coats's chief of staff [[Eric Holcomb]] was a candidate, but withdrew from the race.<ref>{{cite news | last=Joseph | first=Cameron | date=March 26, 2015 | url=https://thehill.com/blogs/ballot-box/237076-indiana-senators-chief-of-staff-launches-campaign-for-his-seat/ | title=Chief of staff launches Senate bid for Coats's seat | work=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]]}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | title=Eric Holcomb drops out of Senate race | url=http://www.theindychannel.com/news/local-news/eric-holcomb-drops-out-of-indiana-senate-race | work=[[WRTV]] | date=February 8, 2016 | access-date=February 11, 2016 | archive-date=February 9, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160209102540/http://www.theindychannel.com/news/local-news/eric-holcomb-drops-out-of-indiana-senate-race | url-status=dead }}</ref>

Former U.S. representative [[Baron Hill (politician)|Baron Hill]] won the Democratic nomination on May 3, but withdrew in July 2016 in favor of [[Evan Bayh]].<ref name="hill">{{cite news | url=http://www.therepublic.com/view/local_story/Baron-Hill-says-he-will-run-fo_1431651799 | title=Baron Hill says he will run for U.S. Senate in 2016 | work=The Republic | location=Columbus, Indiana | date=May 14, 2015 | access-date=May 15, 2015 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150517233036/http://www.therepublic.com/view/local_story/Baron-Hill-says-he-will-run-fo_1431651799 | archive-date=2015-05-17 | url-status=dead }}</ref> Bayh held the seat from 1999 until his retirement in 2011, and also served as governor of Indiana from 1989 to 1997. Former non-profit director John Dickerson also announced he was going to run, but suspended his campaign in early 2016.<ref>{{cite news | last=Groppe | first=Maureen | date=August 15, 2015 | url=http://www.indystar.com/story/news/politics/2014/08/14/john-dickerson-seek-democrat-nod-us-senate/31730779 | title=John Dickerson to seek nod for U.S. Senate | work=Indianapolis Star | access-date=October 14, 2015}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | date=January 21, 2016 | url=http://www.wfyi.org/news/articles/john-dickerson-drops-out-of-race-for-us-senate | title=John Dickerson Drops Out of Race For U.S. Senate | access-date=January 21, 2016}}</ref>

Former Sen. Bayh lost his bid to regain his seat to Rep. Young, who garnered 52% to Bayh's 42%.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Iowa}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Iowa}}
Six-term senator [[Chuck Grassley]] was re-elected with 65% of the vote in 2010. He was 83 years old in 2016. He ran for re-election.<ref name=Grassley>{{cite news | last=Livingston | first=Abby | title=Grassley Planning to Run Again in 2016 | url=http://atr.rollcall.com/grassley-planning-to-run-again-in-2016 | access-date=September 20, 2013 | publisher=[[Roll Call]] | date=September 20, 2013 | archive-date=September 21, 2013 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20130921060632/http://atr.rollcall.com/grassley-planning-to-run-again-in-2016/ | url-status=dead }}</ref><ref name=andhesoff>{{cite web | url=http://www.desmoinesregister.com/story/news/politics/2015/03/30/first-big-fundraiser-will-kick-grassleys-re-election-campaign/70664406 | title=And he's off: Event kicks off Grassley's re-election campaign | work=[[The Des Moines Register]] | last1=Jacobs | first1=Jennifer | last2=Noble | first2=Jason | date=March 31, 2015 | access-date=March 31, 2015}}</ref> Talk radio host Robert Rees announced he was going to challenge Grassley for the nomination,<ref>{{cite news | last=Porter| first=Jake | url=http://iowafreepress.com/2016/01/19/primary-challenger-to-announce-against-grassley | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160125030406/http://iowafreepress.com/2016/01/19/primary-challenger-to-announce-against-grassley/ | url-status=usurped | archive-date=January 25, 2016 | title=Primary Challenger Announces Against Grassley | date=January 19, 2016 | work=Iowa Free Press | access-date=February 18, 2016}}</ref> but later withdrew.<ref>{{cite news | url=http://www.bleedingheartland.com/2016/03/15/ia-sen-robert-rees-ends-gop-primary-challenge-to-chuck-grassley | title=IA-Sen: Robert Rees ends GOP primary challenge to Chuck Grassley | date=March 15, 2016 | work=Bleeding Heartland | access-date=April 19, 2016}}</ref>

Former Lt Governor [[Patty Judge]]<ref name=PattyJudge/> earned the Democratic nomination by defeating State Senator [[Rob Hogg]],<ref name=RobHogg>{{cite news | date=September 23, 2015 | url=http://www.kcrg.com/subject/news/government/cedar-rapids-state-sen-rob-hogg-running-for-us-senate-20150921 | title=Cedar Rapids State Sen. Rob Hogg running for U.S. Senate | work=KCRG 9 | location=Cedar Rapids, Iowa | access-date=September 23, 2015 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150925105923/http://www.kcrg.com/subject/news/government/cedar-rapids-state-sen-rob-hogg-running-for-us-senate-20150921 | archive-date=2015-09-25 | url-status=dead }}</ref> former state senator [[Tom Fiegen]],<ref name=TFiegen>{{cite web | url=http://fiegenforussenate.com | title=Tom Fiegen for US Senate, Iowa | access-date=November 12, 2015}}</ref> and former state representative [[Bob Krause (politician)|Bob Krause]].<ref name=Krause>{{cite web | url=http://www.krauseforiowa.com | title=Bob Krause for Iowa's Future| access-date=February 12, 2016}}</ref> Former state representative [[Ray Zirkelbach]]<ref name=Zirkelbach>{{cite news | last=Lynch | first=James Q. | url=http://www.thegazette.com/subject/news/zirkelbach-is-fourth-candidate-in-democratic-us-senate-race-20151107 | date=November 7, 2015| title=Zirkelbach is fourth candidate in Democratic U.S. Senate race | work=[[The Gazette (Cedar Rapids)|The Gazette]] | access-date=February 20, 2016}}</ref> briefly ran but ended his campaign soon after.

Sen. Grassley won re-election with 60% to Judge's 36%.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Kansas}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Kansas}}
One-term senator [[Jerry Moran]] (Republican) was elected with 70% of the vote in 2010. He was 62 years old in 2016. He ran for re-election.<ref name=timopen/> Radiologist and [[2014 United States Senate election in Kansas|2014 Senate candidate]] [[Milton R. Wolf]] and U.S. representative [[Tim Huelskamp]] declined to run.<ref name=timopen/><ref name=16in16/><ref>{{cite web | url=http://atr.rollcall.com/2014-elections-kansas-primary-pat-roberts-milton-wolf | title=In Kansas, Conservatives Suffer From Mississippi Hangover | date=July 29, 2014 | access-date=September 11, 2014 | work=[[Roll Call]] | first=Alexis | last=Levinson | archive-date=October 4, 2014 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20141004030459/http://atr.rollcall.com/2014-elections-kansas-primary-pat-roberts-milton-wolf/ | url-status=dead }}</ref><ref>{{cite web | url=http://atr.rollcall.com/house-races-2016-tim-huelskamp-wont-challenge-jerry-moran | title=Congressman Says Wife Nixed Senate Run | date=January 8, 2015 | access-date=January 8, 2015 | work=[[Roll Call]] | first=Alexis | last=Levinson | archive-date=January 8, 2015 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150108232536/http://atr.rollcall.com/house-races-2016-tim-huelskamp-wont-challenge-jerry-moran/ | url-status=dead }}</ref>

Patrick Wiesner,<ref name=Wiesner/> an attorney and a candidate for the Senate in [[2010 United States Senate election in Kansas|2010]] and [[2014 United States Senate election in Kansas|2014]], defeated Monique Singh-Bey<ref name=MSingh>{{cite news | last=Mason | first=Tori | url=http://www.wibw.com/home/headlines/Topeka-native-announces-candidacy-for-US-Senate--359660981.html | date=December 1, 2015 | title=Topeka native announces candidacy for U.S. Senate | work=WIBW | access-date=March 10, 2016 | archive-date=March 11, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160311024744/http://www.wibw.com/home/headlines/Topeka-native-announces-candidacy-for-US-Senate--359660981.html | url-status=dead }}</ref> for the Democratic nomination. Potential candidates who declined to run included Wichita Mayor [[Carl Brewer (politician)|Carl Brewer]], 2014 governor nominee [[Paul Davis (Kansas politician)|Paul Davis]], former Kansas City Mayor [[Joe Reardon]], former U.S. representative and [[2008 United States Senate election in Kansas|2008]] nominee [[Jim Slattery]], and [[2014 United States House of Representatives elections in Kansas|2014]] [[Kansas's 2nd congressional district|KS-02]] nominee Margie Wakefield.<ref name=16in16/>

Sen. Moran won re-election with 62% to Wiesner's 32%.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Kentucky}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Kentucky}}
One-term senator [[Rand Paul]] (Republican) was elected with 56% of the vote in 2010. He was 53 years old in 2016. Paul filed for re-election,<ref name="Paul"/> although he was also running for [[president of the United States]] in [[2016 United States presidential election|2016]].<ref>{{cite news | url=https://www.nytimes.com/2015/04/08/us/politics/rand-paul-republican-presidential-nomination.html?_r=0 | title=Rand Paul Announces Presidential Run | work=[[The New York Times]] | date=April 7, 2015 | access-date=April 13, 2015 | last1=Peters | first1=Jeremy W. | first2= Alan | last2=Rappeport}}</ref> Although Kentucky law did not allow for a candidate to appear twice on the same ballot, Paul successfully convinced the Kentucky GOP to adopt a caucus system for 2016, allowing Paul to run for president and for the Senate simultaneously.<ref name=kycaucus>{{cite news | last1=Meyer | first1=Theodoric | title=Kentucky GOP greenlights joint Senate, presidential run for Rand Paul | url=https://www.politico.com/story/2015/08/state-gop-greenlights-joint-senate-presidential-run-for-rand-paul-121634.html | access-date=August 23, 2015 | publisher=[[Politico]] | date=August 22, 2015}}</ref> Kentucky law still bars Paul from appearing twice on the ballot in the general election.<ref name=kycaucus/> However, on February 3, 2016, Paul ended his campaign for the presidency and ran for reelection.<ref>{{cite news | url=http://www.cnn.com/2016/02/03/politics/rand-paul-dropping-out-of-presidential-race/index.html | title=Rand Paul dropping out of presidential race | author1=John King |author2=Manu Raju |author3=Mark Preston | work=cnn.com | access-date=July 25, 2016}}</ref> James Gould and Stephen Slaughter filed to run against Paul.<ref name="SoSKy">{{cite web | url=http://apps.sos.ky.gov/elections/candidatefilings/statewide/default.aspx?id=3 | title=Candidate Filings with the Office of the Secretary of State | publisher=[[Secretary of State of Kentucky]] | url-status=dead | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20151122002230/http://apps.sos.ky.gov/elections/candidatefilings/statewide/default.aspx?id=3 | archive-date=November 22, 2015 }}</ref> Paul won the Republican primary, receiving 169,180 votes (about 85%); James R. Gould received 16,611 (about 8%) and Stephen Howard Slaughter received 13,728 (about 7%).<ref>{{cite web | url=https://elect.ky.gov/results/2010-2019/Documents/2016%20primary%20election%20results.pdf | title=Official 2016 Primary Election Results | publisher=Kentucky Secretary of State | date=May 17, 2016}}</ref>

[[Lexington, Kentucky|Lexington]] Mayor [[Jim Gray (American politician)|Jim Gray]],<ref name=JGray/> Rory Houlihan,<ref name=Houlihan>{{cite news | url=http://bigstory.ap.org/article/3c9d9512cca049bc91dacc645ad77975/democratic-mayor-challenge-gops-rand-paul-senate-race | title=Democratic mayor to challenge GOP's Rand Paul in Senate race | agency=Associated Press | date=January 27, 2016 | access-date=January 27, 2016 | archive-date=January 29, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160129113404/http://bigstory.ap.org/article/3c9d9512cca049bc91dacc645ad77975/democratic-mayor-challenge-gops-rand-paul-senate-race | url-status=dead }}</ref> Ron Leach,<ref name=RLeach>{{cite news | url=http://www.hillbillyreport.org/ron-leach-will-run-for-u-s-senate-in-kentucky | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160201214428/http://www.hillbillyreport.org/ron-leach-will-run-for-u-s-senate-in-kentucky/ | url-status=usurped | archive-date=February 1, 2016 | title=Ron Leach Will Run For U.S. Senate In Kentucky | work=Hillbilly Report | date=January 14, 2016 | access-date=January 26, 2016}}</ref> Sellus Wilder<ref name=SWilder>{{cite news | url=http://www.kentucky.com/news/politics-government/article56605288.html | title=Lexington Mayor Jim Gray running against U.S. Sen. Rand Paul | work=Lexington Herald-Leader | date=January 27, 2016 | access-date=January 27, 2016}}</ref> Jeff Kender, Tom Recktenwald (who was a candidate in [[2014 United States Senate election in Kentucky|2014]]), and Grant Short ran for the Democratic nomination.<ref name="SoSKy"/> Gray won the nomination.

Paul won re-election with 57% of the vote to Gray's 43%.
