{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Louisiana}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Louisiana}}
Two-term senator [[David Vitter]] (Republican) was re-elected with 57% of the vote in 2010. After losing the 2015 gubernatorial race, Vitter chose to retire from the Senate at the end of his term.<ref name=RCRG/><ref>{{cite news | url=https://abcnews.go.com/Politics/wireStory/latest-vitter-run-us-senate-2016-35350969 | title=The Latest: Vitter Says Won't Run for US Senate in 2016 | work=ABC News | date=November 21, 2015 | access-date=November 21, 2015 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20151122202500/https://abcnews.go.com/Politics/wireStory/latest-vitter-run-us-senate-2016-35350969 | archive-date=November 22, 2015 | url-status=dead | df=mdy-all }}</ref>

Republicans who ran for the seat included U.S. representatives [[Charles Boustany]]<ref name=Boustany>{{cite news | last=Berry | first=Deborah | date=November 25, 2015 | url=http://www.theadvertiser.com/story/news/local/louisiana/2015/11/23/rep-charles-boustany-run-senate/76257716 | title=Rep. Charles Boustany will run for Senate | work=The Advertiser | access-date=December 9, 2015}}</ref> and [[John Fleming (U.S. politician)|John Fleming]],<ref name=Fleming>{{cite news | last=O'Donoghue | first=Julia | date=December 7, 2015 | url=http://www.nola.com/politics/index.ssf/2015/12/louisiana_congressman_john_fle.html | archive-url=https://archive.today/20161023081658/http://www.nola.com/politics/index.ssf/2015/12/louisiana_congressman_john_fle.html | url-status=dead | archive-date=October 23, 2016 | title=Louisiana Congressman John Fleming launches bid for David Vitter's Senate seat | work=[[The Times-Picayune]] | access-date=December 9, 2015}}</ref> former U.S. representative [[Joseph Cao]],<ref name=Cao>{{cite news | author=Richard Rainey | date=December 16, 2015 | work=[[The Times-Picayune]] | access-date=January 14, 2016 | url=http://www.nola.com/politics/index.ssf/2015/12/joseph_cao_enters_senate_race.html | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20151219064350/http://www.nola.com/politics/index.ssf/2015/12/joseph_cao_enters_senate_race.html | url-status=dead | archive-date=December 19, 2015 | title=Joseph Cao enters Senate race for Vitter's seat, tells supporters by email}}</ref> State Treasurer [[John Kennedy (Louisiana politician)|John Kennedy]],<ref name=Neely/> retired U.S. Air Force Colonel Rob Maness,<ref name=Maness>{{cite news | url=http://www.wwl.com/Maness-officially-joins-Senate-race/22415681 | title=Maness officially joins Senate race | work=WWL AM870 | date=January 29, 2016 | access-date=January 29, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160203221037/http://www.wwl.com/Maness-officially-joins-Senate-race/22415681 | archive-date=February 3, 2016 | url-status=dead | df=mdy-all }}</ref> and former Louisiana state representative [[David Duke]]. Other potential Republican candidates included [[Louisiana Public Service Commission|Public Service Commissioner]] [[Erik Skrmetta]],<ref name=JAlford>{{cite news | last=Alford| first=Jeremy | date=December 22, 2015 | url=https://www.businessreport.com/article/possibilities-surface-louisianas-u-s-senate-race | title=More possibilities surface for Louisiana's US Senate race | work=Greater Baton Rouge Business Report | access-date=February 4, 2016}}</ref> [[2014 United States House of Representatives elections in Louisiana|2014]] candidate for [[Louisiana's 5th congressional district|LA-05]] Zach Dasher,<ref name=JAlford/> state representative [[Paul Hollis]],<ref>{{cite news | last=Alford | first=Jeremy | date=December 7, 2015 | access-date=March 4, 2016 | url=http://www.houmatoday.com/article/20151207/ARTICLES/151209734 | title=Names floating in, out of developing US Senate race | work=Houma Today | archive-date=December 10, 2015 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20151210181833/http://www.houmatoday.com/article/20151207/ARTICLES/151209734 | url-status=dead }}</ref> and former president of [[Jefferson Parish, Louisiana|Jefferson Parish]] John Young.<ref>{{cite news | last=Tidmore | first=Christopher | date=December 7, 2015 | url=http://www.louisianaweekly.com/young-cazayoux-and-hebert-consider-vitters-u-s-senate-seat | title=Young, Cazayoux and Hebert consider Vitter's U.S. Senate seat | work=[[The Louisiana Weekly]] | access-date=March 4, 2016}}</ref>

Democratic candidates included [[Louisiana Public Service Commission|Public Service Commissioner]] [[Foster Campbell]],<ref name=Campbell/> attorney Derrick Edwards,<ref name=DEdwards>{{cite news | last= Alford | first=Jeremy | url=http://theind.com/article-22906-new-names-still-hover-over-senate-race.html | title=New names still hover over Senate race | date=March 23, 2016 | work=[[The Independent]] | access-date=April 20, 2016}}</ref> Caroline Fayard, an attorney and candidate for lieutenant governor in [[2010 Louisiana state elections|2010]],<ref name=Fayard>{{cite news | last=Crisp | first=Elizabeth | date=February 4, 2016 | work=[[The Advocate (Louisiana)|The Advocate]] | url=http://theadvocate.com/news/14779292-123/caroline-fayard-launches-us-senate-bid | title=Democrat Caroline Fayard launches U.S. Senate bid | access-date=February 5, 2016 | archive-date=February 5, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160205070328/http://theadvocate.com/news/14779292-123/caroline-fayard-launches-us-senate-bid | url-status=dead }}</ref> and businessman Josh Pellerin.<ref name=Pellerin>{{cite web | last=Stickney | first=Ken | url=http://www.theadvertiser.com/story/news/2016/03/28/pellerin-announcement-comes-april-5/82362142 | title=Pellerin announcement comes April 5 | date=March 28, 2016 | work=[[The Daily Advertiser (Lafayette)|The Daily Advertiser]] | access-date=April 20, 2016}}</ref> Other potential Democratic candidates included state legislators [[Robert Johnson (Louisiana politician)|Robert Johnson]], [[Eric LaFleur]], and [[Gary Smith Jr.]], and Mayor of [[Alexandria, Louisiana|Alexandria]] [[Jacques Roy (mayor)|Jacques Roy]].<ref>{{cite news | last=Stickney | first=Ken | date=January 25, 2016 | url=http://www.theadvertiser.com/story/news/2016/01/25/pellerin-explore-senate-campaign/79319780 | title=Pellerin to explore Senate campaign | work=[[The Daily Advertiser (Lafayette)|The Daily Advertiser]] | access-date=March 4, 2016}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | last=Hilburn | first=Greg | url=http://www.thenewsstar.com/story/news/2016/01/06/alexandria-mayor-state-rep-johnson-consider-senate-run/78356984 | title=Alexandria Mayor, state Rep. Johnson consider Senate run | date=January 6, 2016 | work=The News-Star}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | last=Ballard | first=Mark | url=http://theadvocate.com/news/opinion/14328336-63/political-horizons-despite-campaign-fatigue-a-senate-race-looms | title=Political Horizons: Despite campaign fatigue, a crowded, close Senate race looms | date=December 19, 2015 | work=[[The Advocate (Louisiana)|The Advocate]] | access-date=February 4, 2016 | archive-date=January 15, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160115111630/http://theadvocate.com/news/opinion/14328336-63/political-horizons-despite-campaign-fatigue-a-senate-race-looms | url-status=dead }}</ref> Former U.S. senator [[Mary Landrieu]] and her brother, [[New Orleans, Louisiana|New Orleans]] Mayor [[Mitch Landrieu]], declined to run.<ref>{{cite news | last1=Rainey | first1=Richard | title=Louisiana Senate race: who's in, who's out in the early going | date=December 10, 2015 | url=http://www.nola.com/politics/index.ssf/2015/12/louisiana_senate_race_whos_in.html | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20151212203611/http://www.nola.com/politics/index.ssf/2015/12/louisiana_senate_race_whos_in.html | url-status=dead | archive-date=December 12, 2015 | access-date=December 16, 2015 | agency=The Times-Picayune | publisher=2015 NOLA Media Group}}</ref>

As no candidate won a majority of the vote in the "[[Louisiana primary|jungle primary]]", a runoff election was held on December 10 to choose between Kennedy and Campbell (the 2 candidates with the most votes in the primary).<ref name="mettler1">{{cite news | last1=Mettler | first1=Katie | title=Republican, Democrat head to runoff in Louisiana Senate race | url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/politics/2016/live-updates/general-election/real-time-updates-on-the-2016-election-voting-and-race-results/republican-democrat-head-to-runoff-in-louisiana-senate-race | access-date=November 9, 2016 | newspaper=The Washington Post | date=November 9, 2016}}</ref>  John Kennedy was declared the winner of the runoff election with 61% of the vote to Campbell's 39%.

== Maryland ==

Five-term U.S. senator [[Barbara Mikulski]] of the Democratic Party was re-elected with 62% of the vote in 2010. She is the longest-serving female senator and the longest-serving woman in the history of the U.S. Congress. She did not seek re-election.<ref name="complicate">{{Cite news |last=Bolton |first=Alexander |date=November 28, 2014 |title=2016 retirements could complicate Dems' comeback plans |work=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] |url=https://thehill.com/homenews/campaign/225488-2016-retirements-could-complicate-dems-comeback-plans/ |access-date=December 1, 2014}}</ref>

The candidates who filed for the Democratic nomination were U.S. representatives [[Donna Edwards]]<ref name="donnaedwards">{{Cite news |date=March 10, 2015 |title=Edwards jumps into Maryland Senate race |work=[[Politico]] |url=https://www.politico.com/story/2015/03/donna-edwards-maryland-senate-mikulski-seat-115937 |access-date=May 16, 2015}}</ref> and [[Chris Van Hollen]],<ref name=vanhollen/> Freddie Donald Dickson Jr., Ralph Jaffe, Theresa Scaldaferri, Charles Smith, Violate Staley, Blaine Taylor, Ed Tinus, and Lih Young.<ref name="mdboardofelections">{{Cite web |title=Maryland: The State Board of Election, 2016 candidate listing |url=http://elections.state.md.us/elections/2016/primary_candidates/gen_cand_lists_2016_3_007-.html |access-date=February 3, 2016}}</ref> Van Hollen won the April 26 primary.

The Republican candidates who filed were former [[United States Assistant Secretary of Defense|Deputy Assistant Secretary of Defense]] and Senate candidate in [[2012 United States Senate election in Maryland|2012]] Richard Douglas,<ref name="fritze">{{Cite news |last=Fritze |first=John |date=August 24, 2015 |title=Richard Douglas first Republican to enter Md. Senate race |work=[[The Baltimore Sun]] |url=https://www.baltimoresun.com/2015/08/24/richard-douglas-first-republican-to-enter-md-senate-race/ |access-date=December 1, 2015 |archive-date=September 24, 2015 |archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150924003346/http://www.baltimoresun.com/news/maryland/politics/blog/bal-douglas-first-republican-to-enter-md-senate-race-20150824-story.html |url-status=live }}</ref> Chrys Kefalas,<ref name="Kefalas">{{Cite news |date=May 18, 2015 |title=Openly gay Republican explores bid for Senate seat from Md. |url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/local/md-politics/openly-gay-republican-launches-candidacy-for-senate-seat-from-md/2015/05/18/3ad9f61a-fd79-11e4-833c-a2de05b6b2a4_story.html |access-date=October 29, 2015}}</ref> State Delegate [[Kathy Szeliga]],<ref name=Szeliga/> Chris Chaffee, Sean Connor, John Graziani, Greg Holmes, Joseph David Hooe, Mark McNicholas, Lynn Richardson, Anthony Seda, Richard Shawver, Dave Walle, and Garry T. Yarrington.<ref name=mdboardofelections/> Szeliga won the primary to face Van Hollen in the general election.

Rep. Van Hollen won election to the Senate with 61% of the vote to Szeliga's 36%.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Missouri}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Missouri}}
One-term senator [[Roy Blunt]] (Republican) was elected with 54% of the vote in 2010. He was 66 years old in 2016. He ran for re-election.<ref name=bluntcurve/> Former U.S. representative and [[2012 United States Senate election in Missouri|2012 Senate nominee]] [[Todd Akin]] was rumored to be a possible candidate, but declined to run.<ref>{{cite news | title=Akin won't rule out 2016 run against Blunt | url=http://www.news-leader.com/story/news/politics/2014/07/15/akin-rule-run-blunt/12660531 | first=Jim | last= Stinson| publisher=Springfield News-Leader | date=July 15, 2014 | access-date=November 13, 2014}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | title=Akin: I'm not running for Senate | url=https://www.politico.com/story/2015/02/todd-akin-senate-missouri-2016-elections-115535.html?hp=l7_4 | first=Kendall | last= Breitman | publisher=[[Politico]] | date=February 26, 2015 | access-date=February 26, 2015}}</ref> Three candidates ran against Blunt for the Republican nomination, the best-known being sales manager, Tea Party activist, and [[2010 United States Senate election in Missouri|2010]] candidate Kristin Nichols, but Blunt won decisively with 72% of the vote.

For the Democrats, Secretary of State [[Jason Kander]]<ref name=Kander/> easily won the nomination, defeating Robert Mack, Pastor [[Cori Bush]],<ref name=CoriBush>{{cite news | last=Rivas | first=Rebecca | date=February 11, 2016 | url=http://www.stlamerican.com/news/local_news/article_7e0f60a8-d057-11e5-b969-f37bbb2e9a82.html | title=Black challengers of Democrat incumbents fight with party over access | work=[[The St. Louis American]] | access-date=February 16, 2016}}</ref><ref>{{cite web | title=Cori Bush for U.S. Senate | url=http://coribush.com | access-date=February 16, 2016 | archive-date=February 13, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160213144159/http://coribush.com/ | url-status=dead }}</ref> and activist Chief Wana Dubie.<ref name=WanaDubie>{{cite news | last=Sheeley | first=Andrew | url=http://m.thesalemnewsonline.com/mobile/news/article_43a09af8-dbcf-11e5-bb0c-ffca39b10849.html | title=Wana Dubie officially first US Senate candidate from Salem | date=February 25, 2016 | work=The Salem News | access-date=February 27, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160229215845/http://m.thesalemnewsonline.com/mobile/news/article_43a09af8-dbcf-11e5-bb0c-ffca39b10849.html | archive-date=February 29, 2016 | url-status=dead | df=mdy-all }}</ref> [[Governor of Missouri|Governor]] [[Jay Nixon]] and [[State Treasurer of Missouri|State Treasurer]] [[Clint Zweifel]] chose not to seek election to the Senate.<ref>{{cite news | last=Cillizilla | first=Chris | title=Missouri Gov. Jay Nixon steps forward, but toward what? | url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/blogs/the-fix/wp/2013/09/19/missouri-gov-jay-nixon-steps-forward-but-toward-what | access-date=April 8, 2014 | newspaper=The Washington Post | date=September 19, 2013}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | url=http://www.kansascity.com/news/government-politics/article3992608.html | title=Missouri Treasurer Zweifel won't run for office | newspaper=Kansas City Star | date=November 18, 2013 | access-date=November 19, 2013}}</ref>

Sen. Blunt won re-election with 49% of the vote to Kander's 46%.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Nevada}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Nevada}}
Five-term senator and [[Party leaders of the United States Senate|Senate Minority Leader]] [[Harry Reid]] (Democrat) was re-elected with 50% of the vote in 2010. Reid did not seek re-election.<ref name="Carl Hulse">{{cite news | last=Hulse | first=Carl | date=March 27, 2015 | url=https://www.nytimes.com/2015/03/28/us/politics/senator-harry-reid-retire.html?_r=0 | title=Harry Reid Says He Won't Seek Re-election | newspaper=The New York Times}}</ref> Former [[Nevada Attorney General]] [[Catherine Cortez Masto]] earned the Democratic nomination, defeating Bobby Mahendra, Liddo Susan O'Briant, and Allen Rheinhart in the primary on June 14, 2016.

Congressman [[Joe Heck]]<ref name=JoeHeck/> defeated eight candidates, including [[2010 United States Senate election in Nevada|2010]] nominee [[Sharron Angle]],<ref name=Angle>{{cite news | last=Kudialis | first=Chris | url=http://lasvegassun.com/news/2016/mar/18/sharron-angle-to-make-encore-bid-for-reids-senate | title=Angle to make another bid for Reid's Senate seat | date=March 18, 2016 | work=[[Las Vegas Sun]] | access-date=April 20, 2016}}</ref> who ran against Reid in 2010, for the Republican nomination.

Jarrod M. Williams, an independent candidate ran for the seat. He describes himself as a [[Democratic Socialist]], a supporter of [[Bernie Sanders]], and a member of the [[Socialist Party USA]], although the party does not have a chapter in the State of [[Nevada]].{{citation needed|date=September 2016}}
Cortez Masto was elected with 47.1% of the vote to Heck's 44.7%.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in New Hampshire}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from New Hampshire}}
One-term senator [[Kelly Ayotte]] (Republican) was elected with 60% of the vote in 2010. She was 48 years old in 2016. Ayotte ran for re-election.<ref name=seekre/> [[Jim Rubens]], a former state senator, candidate for governor in [[1998 New Hampshire gubernatorial election|1998]] and for the Senate in [[2014 United States Senate election in New Hampshire|2014]], announced a challenge to Ayotte in the primary,<ref name=Rubens>{{cite news | url=http://nhpr.org/post/ayotte-faces-primary-challenge-former-gop-state-senator | title=Ayotte Faces Primary Challenge From Former GOP State Senator | work=nhpr | date=March 8, 2016 | access-date=March 9, 2016}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | url=http://www.unionleader.com/Jim-Rubens-to-challenge-incumbent-Sen.-Ayotte-in-GOP-primary | title=Jim Rubens to challenge incumbent Sen. Ayotte in GOP primary | work=[[New Hampshire Union-Leader]] | date=March 5, 2016 | access-date=March 9, 2016 | archive-date=March 6, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160306110813/http://www.unionleader.com/Jim-Rubens-to-challenge-incumbent-Sen.-Ayotte-in-GOP-primary | url-status=dead }}</ref> but Ayotte won the nomination.

[[Brian Chabot]] was the Libertarian candidate for US Senate in [[2016 United States Senate election in New Hampshire|2016]]. He was a relative newcomer to politics, having run for US Senate in 2010 and US representative in 2014.

[[Governor of New Hampshire|Governor]] [[Maggie Hassan]] ran for the Democratic nomination.<ref name=Hassan/> Other potential candidates included [[Executive Council of New Hampshire|Executive Councilor]] [[Chris Pappas (American politician)|Chris Pappas]], state senators [[Dan Feltes]] and [[Donna Soucy]], [[Portsmouth, New Hampshire|Portsmouth]] city councilor and daughter of U.S. senator [[Jeanne Shaheen]] Stefany Shaheen, and campaign manager for Senator Shaheen Mike Vlacich.<ref>{{cite news | last=DiSasto | first=John | date=November 15, 2014 | url=http://nhjournal.com/analysis-nh-democrats-already-have-deep-bench-for-top-races-in-2016 | title=Analysis: NH Democrats already have deep bench for top races in 2016 | work=NHJournal}}</ref>

[[2016 United States Senate election in New Hampshire#Polling 2|A series of polls]] taken by WMUR/UNH in February, April, and July 2016, as well as WBUR polls taken in May and July/August, showed Hassan gaining support over time and subsequently leading Ayotte.

Gov. Hassan won a very close election, 353,978 or 47.97%, to Sen. Ayotte's 353,262 or 47.87%, a difference of 716 votes. Sen. Ayotte conceded the race to Gov. Hassan around noon on Wednesday, November 9, 2016.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in New York}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from New York}}
Three-term senator [[Chuck Schumer]] (Democrat) was re-elected with 66% of the vote in 2010. He was 66 years old in 2016. He ran for re-election.<ref name=RCRG/> Schumer had been elected leader of the [[Senate Democratic Caucus|Senate Democrats]] on November 16, 2016, succeeding [[Harry Reid]].<ref>{{cite web | author=Ed O'eefe | author2=Mike DeBonis | name-list-style=amp | url=https://www.bostonglobe.com/news/politics/2016/11/16/chuck-schumer-elected-succeed-harry-reid-senate-minority-leader/D4284OThDQcJfsrR5XkWoK/story.html | title=Democrats shake up Senate leadership | publisher=Bostonglobe.com | date=November 16, 2016 | access-date=August 25, 2017}}</ref><ref>{{Cite news|url=https://www.theguardian.com/us-news/2016/nov/16/chuck-schumer-senate-democrats-elect-minority-leader|title=Chuck Schumer elected new minority leader by Senate Democrats|last=Gambino|first=Lauren|date=2016-11-16|work=The Guardian|access-date=2019-08-31|language=en-GB|issn=0261-3077}}</ref>

[[Wendy E. Long|Wendy Long]], the Republican nominee in [[2012 United States Senate election in New York|2012]], ran as the nominee of Republican, Conservative, and Reform Parties.<ref name=WLong/> Other potential Republican candidates included U.S. representatives [[Chris Gibson (New York politician)|Chris Gibson]] and [[Peter T. King]].<ref name=challengeschumer>{{cite news | url=https://nypost.com/2015/04/06/top-republican-candidates-to-challenge-chuck-schumer | title=Top Republican candidates to challenge Chuck Schumer | work=[[New York Post]] | last=Dicker | first=Fredric U. | date=April 6, 2015 | access-date=April 7, 2015}}</ref> U.S. representative [[Richard L. Hanna]], Manhattan Republican Party Chairwoman Adele Malpass, and former [[CNBC]] television host [[Lawrence Kudlow|Larry Kudlow]]<ref name="Johnson">{{cite web | url=http://www.nationalreview.com/article/420242/larry-kudlow-nrsc-senate-bid-ny-ct | work=[[National Review]] | title=Larry Kudlow and NRSC Renew Discussions on Senate Run | date=June 24, 2015 | access-date=June 25, 2015 | first=Eliana | last=Johnson}}</ref> were also mentioned as possible candidates, but all declined to run.<ref name=challengeschumer/><ref>{{cite news | url=http://www.syracuse.com/news/index.ssf/2015/04/us_rep_richard_hanna_says_he_wont_run_against_chuck_schumer_in_2016_ny_senate_ra.html | title=U.S. Rep. Richard Hanna says he won't run against Chuck Schumer in 2016 NY senate race | work=Syracuse.com | last=Weiner | first=Mark | date=April 7, 2015 | access-date=April 7, 2015}}</ref>

Robin Laverne Wilson, the [[Green Party of New York]] nominee, received 1.5% of the vote.<ref name="NYSenateResults">{{cite web | url=https://www.nytimes.com/elections/results/new-york | title=New York Election Results 2016 | website=[[The New York Times]] | access-date=November 16, 2016}}</ref> Alex Merced, the [[Libertarian Party (United States)|Libertarian Party]] candidate,<ref>{{Cite news | url=http://www.pressconnects.com/story/news/politics/2016/11/03/us-senate-race-schumer-long-square/93258712 | title=U.S. SENATE RACE: Schumer, Long square off | newspaper=[[Press & Sun-Bulletin]] | date=November 3, 2016}}</ref> received 0.7% of the vote.<ref name="NYSenateResults"/>

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in North Carolina}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from North Carolina}}
Two-term senator [[Richard Burr]] (Republican) was re-elected with 55% of the vote in 2010. He was 61 years old in 2016. There had been speculation that Burr might retire,<ref name=NCFT>{{cite news | last=Cahn | first=Emily | title=Opportunities Now and Later in North Carolina | url=http://www.rollcall.com/news/opportunities_now_and_later_in_north_carolina-229628-1.html | access-date=December 11, 2013 | publisher=[[Roll Call]] | date=December 11, 2013 | archive-date=December 15, 2013 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131215231214/http://www.rollcall.com/news/opportunities_now_and_later_in_north_carolina-229628-1.html | url-status=dead }}</ref> but he ran for re-election.<ref name="rumorsaside"/><ref>{{cite news | url=http://www.wncn.com/story/27776263/sen-burr-confirms-he-will-run-in-2016 | title=Sen. Burr confirms he will run in 2016 | publisher=WNCN | date=January 6, 2015 | access-date=January 8, 2015 | archive-date=January 8, 2015 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150108150731/http://www.wncn.com/story/27776263/sen-burr-confirms-he-will-run-in-2016 | url-status=dead }}</ref>

Three Republicans challenged Burr in the primary: [[Greg Brannon]],<ref name=GBrannon>{{cite news | author=Colin Campbell | url=http://www.newsobserver.com/news/politics-government/politics-columns-blogs/under-the-dome/article50894050.html
 | title=Republican Greg Brannon challenges Richard Burr in second Senate bid | work=[[The News & Observer]] | date=December 21, 2015| access-date=December 31, 2015}}</ref> Larry Holmquist,<ref name="Holmquist">{{cite news | author=Sarah Krueger | date=September 24, 2015 | url=http://myfox8.com/2015/09/24/larry-holmquist-enters-senate-race-challenging-richard-burr | title=Larry Holmquist enters Senate race, challenging Richard Burr | access-date=December 1, 2015}}</ref> and former [[North Carolina Superior Court|Superior Court]] judge Paul Wright.<ref name="PWright">{{cite news | url=http://www.citizen-times.com/story/news/2015/09/11/former-judge-wright-running-burrs-senate-seat/72098026 | title=Former judge Wright running for Burr's Senate seat | work=[[Asheville Citizen-Times]] | date=September 11, 2015| access-date=December 1, 2015}}</ref> On March 15, Burr won the primary with 61% of the vote.<ref name=":2">{{cite web | title=NC SBE Contest Results | url=http://er.ncsbe.gov/contest_details.html?election_dt=03/15/2016&county_id=0&contest_id=1005 | access-date=March 18, 2016}}</ref>

Former state representative [[Deborah K. Ross|Deborah Ross]],<ref name=ross1/> [[Spring Lake, North Carolina|Spring Lake]] Mayor Chris Rey,<ref name=ChrisRey>{{cite news | last=Woolverton | first=Paul | date=September 21, 2015 | work=[[Fayetteville Observer]] | title=Spring Lake Mayor Chris Rey announces run for US Senate | url=http://www.fayobserver.com/blogs/news/peoples_business/spring-lake-mayor-chris-rey-announces-run-for-us-senate/article_943e3dc8-605d-11e5-a8de-f3a10ca97579.html | access-date=September 21, 2015}}</ref> businessman Kevin Griffin,<ref name=KevinGriffin>{{cite news | last=Leslie | first=Laura | url=http://www.wral.com/durham-businessman-joining-dem-field-in-us-senate-race/14972023 | title=Durham businessman joining Dem field in US Senate race | work=WRAL | date=October 15, 2015}}</ref> and retired U.S. Army Captain Ernest Reeves<ref name=Reeves>{{cite web| url=http://www.ernestreeves.com/| title=Ernest Reeves for U.S. Senate| access-date=December 31, 2015| archive-date=December 27, 2015| archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20151227002406/http://www.ernestreeves.com/| url-status=dead}}</ref> ran for the Democratic nomination. Former U.S. senator [[Kay Hagan]],<ref name=hagan>{{cite news | last=Cillizza | first=Chris | title=Democrats' Senate hopes take a hit with Kay Hagan's no-go decision | url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/blogs/the-fix/wp/2015/06/24/democrats-senate-hopes-take-a-hit-with-kay-hagans-no-go-decision | access-date=June 30, 2015 | newspaper=The Washington Post | date=June 24, 2015}}</ref> state treasurer [[Janet Cowell]],<ref>{{cite news | url=http://www.newsobserver.com/news/politics-government/politics-columns-blogs/under-the-dome/article18524942.html | title=Cowell seeking third term as NC treasurer | first=Craig | last=Jarvis | newspaper=[[The News & Observer]] | date=April 14, 2015| access-date=April 14, 2015}}</ref> and [[Anthony Foxx]], the [[United States Secretary of Transportation]] and former [[Mayor of Charlotte, North Carolina|mayor]] of [[Charlotte, North Carolina|Charlotte]], declined to run.<ref>{{cite magazine | url=http://washingtonexaminer.com/foxx-wont-run-for-senate-in-2016/article/2553566 | title=Foxx won't run for Senate in 2016| first=Kelly | last=Cohen | magazine=[[The Washington Examiner]]| date=September 17, 2014 | access-date=October 2, 2014}}</ref> On March 15, Ross won the primary with 62% of the vote.<ref name=":1">{{cite web | url=http://er.ncsbe.gov/contest_details.html?election_dt=03/15/2016&county_id=0&contest_id=1004 | title=NC SBE Contest Results| access-date=March 18, 2016}}</ref>

Burr won re-election 51% to 45% for Ross.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in North Dakota}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from North Dakota}}
One-term senator [[John Hoeven]] (Republican) was elected with 76% of the vote in 2010. He was 59 years old in 2016. He ran for re-election.<ref name=heitkampeyeing/>

Democrats endorsed state representative [[Eliot Glassheim]].<ref>{{cite news | last1=Nowatzki | first1=Mike | title=ND Democrat Glassheim says he's healthy and ready to run for US Senate | url=http://www.inforum.com/news/legislature/4000749-nd-democrat-glassheim-says-hes-healthy-and-ready-run-us-senate | access-date=April 4, 2016 | work=[[The Forum of Fargo-Moorhead]] | publisher=[[Forum Communications]] | date=April 2, 2016}}</ref> On November 7, 2015, the Libertarian party nominated Robert Marquette.

Hoeven defeated Glassheim 78% to 17%.

== Ohio ==

{{Infobox election
| election_name      = Ohio election
| country            = Ohio
| type               = presidential
| ongoing            = no

| nominee1           = '''[[Rob Portman]]'''
| party1             = Republican Party (United States)

| image2             = Ted Strickland photo.jpg
| nominee2           = [[Ted Strickland]]
| party2             = Democratic Party (United States)
| popular_vote2      = 1,996,908
| percentage2        = 37.2%

| map = {{switcher
|[[file:2016 United States Senate election in Ohio results map by county.svg|210px]]
|County results 
|default=1
}}
| map_caption = '''Portman:''' {{legend0|#ffb2b2|40–50%}} {{legend0|#e27f7f|50–60%}} {{legend0|#d75d5d|60–70%}} {{legend0|#d72f30|70–80%}} {{legend0|#C21B18|80–90%}}<br> '''Strickland:''' {{legend0|#a5b0ff|40–50%}} {{legend0|#7996e2|50–60%}}
| title              = U.S. Senator
| before_election    = [[Rob Portman]]
| before_party       = Republican Party (United States)
| after_election     = [[Rob Portman]]
| after_party        = Republican Party (United States)

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Ohio}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Ohio}}
One-term senator [[Rob Portman]] (Republican) was elected with 57% of the vote in 2010. He was 60 years old in 2016. He ran for re-election. He had considered running for president, but decided not to.<ref name=wontpotus/>

Two candidates filed to challenge him: Don Elijah Eckhart, who ran for [[Ohio's 15th congressional district|OH-15]] as an independent in [[2008 United States House of Representatives elections in Ohio|2008]],<ref name=Eckhart>{{cite news | last=Torry | first=Jack | date=May 19, 2015| title=Portman has conservative primary challenger | url=http://www.dispatch.com/content/blogs/the-daily-briefing/2015/05/eckhart-5-19-2015.html | work=[[The Columbus Dispatch]]}}</ref> and Melissa Strzala. However, Strzala was disqualified.<ref>{{cite news | last=Johnson| first=Alan | url=http://www.dispatch.com/content/stories/local/2015/12/31/martin-omalley-primary-ballot.html | title=Martin O'Malley won't appear on Ohio's primary ballot | work=[[The Columbus Dispatch]] | date=January 1, 2016 | access-date=January 14, 2016}}</ref> On March 15, Portman won the primary with 82% of the vote.

Former [[Governor of Ohio|governor]] and Congressman [[Ted Strickland]], [[City Council of Cincinnati elections|Cincinnati City Councilman]] [[P.G. Sittenfeld]], and occupational therapist Kelli Prather ran for the Democratic nomination.<ref name=pgyes>{{cite news | url=http://www.cincinnati.com/story/news/politics/2015/01/22/us-senate-sittenfeld-takes-portman/22126849 | title=Sittenfeld: Yes, I'm running for U.S. Senate | work=[[The Cincinnati Enquirer]] | last=Coolidge | first=Sharon | access-date=January 22, 2015}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | last1=Wehrman | first1=Jessica | last2=Torry | first2=Jack | title=Ted Strickland says swift GOP attacks in Senate race are a 'compliment' | url=http://www.dispatch.com/content/stories/local/2015/02/24/former-gov.-ted-strickland-running-for-senate.html | access-date=March 9, 2015 | newspaper=The Columbus Dispatch | date=February 25, 2015}}</ref><ref name=Prather>{{cite news | last1=Rowland | first1=Darrell| last2=Ludlow | first2=Randy | title=Kasich joins GOP competitors in filing for Ohio primary | date=December 16, 2015|
url=http://www.dispatch.com/content/stories/local/2015/12/16/primary-2016-filing-deadline.html | work=[[The Columbus Dispatch]]}}</ref> Former state representative [[Bob Hagan]] had filed papers to run,<ref name=Hagan>{{cite news | last=Vardon | first=Joe | title=Ohio legislator to challenge Portman in '16 | url=http://www.dispatch.com/content/stories/local/2013/05/07/State-rep-files-paperwork-to-oppose-Portman-in-2016.html | access-date=September 20, 2013 | newspaper=The Columbus Dispatch | date=May 8, 2013}}</ref> but later withdrew from the race.<ref>{{cite news | url=http://www.dispatch.com/content/stories/local/2015/01/02/who-will-portman-face-in-16.html | title=Who will face Portman in '16? | work=The Columbus Dispatch | date=January 2, 2015 | access-date=January 10, 2015}}</ref> On March 15, Strickland won the primary with 65% of the vote.

Joseph DeMare, a machinist from Bowling Green, was the Green Party candidate. He ran unopposed in the March 15, 2016 primary, and received enough votes to substantially increase the number of enrolled Green Party members. In Ohio, the only way to join a political party is to vote in that party's primary.

Portman defeated Strickland 58% to 37.2%.

== Oklahoma ==

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Oklahoma}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Oklahoma}}
Two-term senator [[Tom Coburn]] (Republican) was re-elected with 71% of the vote in 2010, but chose to leave office before the end of his term after being diagnosed with [[prostate cancer]]. [[James Lankford]] won the [[2014 United States Senate special election in Oklahoma|2014 special election]] to serve the remainder of Coburn's term.<ref name=OkCan>{{cite news | last=Taylor | first=Jessica | title=Crowded primary likely in race for Coburn seat | url=https://thehill.com/blogs/ballot-box/senate-races/195783-crowded-primary-likely-in-race-for-coburn-seat/ | access-date=January 17, 2014 | newspaper=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] | date=January 17, 2014}}</ref> Lankford ran for re-election.<ref name=RCRG/>

Former Congressman [[Dan Boren]] was viewed by some Oklahoma political operatives as the only Democrat who could make the 2016 race competitive, but was seen as unlikely to run.<ref name="Oklahoma Farm Team">{{cite news | url=http://kfor.com/2014/11/04/u-s-senate-seat-goes-to-republican-james-lankford | title=U.S. Senate seat goes to Republican James Lankford | date=November 4, 2014 | publisher=[[Roll Call]] | access-date=November 21, 2014}}</ref> Lankford's [[2014 United States Senate special election in Oklahoma|2014 special election]] opponent [[Constance N. Johnson]] had also declined to run.<ref name="Oklahoma Farm Team"/>

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Oregon}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Oregon}}
Three-term senator [[Ron Wyden]] (Democrat) was re-elected with 57% of the vote in 2010. He was 67 years old in 2016. He ran for re-election.<ref name=RCRG/>

[[Medford, Oregon|Medford]] City Councilor Kevin Stine<ref name=stine>{{cite web | last=Mann | first=Damian | date=September 21, 2015 | url=http://www.mailtribune.com/article/20150921/NEWS/150929984 | title=Medford Councilor Stine to challenge Sen. Ron Wyden | work=[[Mail Tribune]] | access-date=September 21, 2015 | archive-date=October 1, 2015 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20151001203745/http://www.mailtribune.com/article/20150921/NEWS/150929984 | url-status=dead }}</ref> and retired locomotive engineer Paul Weaver<ref name=SReynolds>{{cite news | date=November 17, 2015 | work=[[The Oregonian]] | access-date=March 9, 2016 | url=http://www.oregonlive.com/politics/index.ssf/2015/11/whos_running_for_president_ore.html | title=Who's running for president, Oregon governor, other seats? The latest on 2016 candidates}}</ref> challenged Wyden for the Democratic nomination. Wyden won the Democratic nomination.

Information technology consultant and [[2014 United States Senate election in Oregon|2014]] candidate Mark Callahan,<ref name=callahan/> businessman Sam Carpenter,<ref name=Carpenter>{{cite news | url=http://www.bendbulletin.com/home/3969938-151/bend-businessman-carpenter-to-run-for-senate?referrer=bullet5 | title=Bend businessman Carpenter to run for Senate | last=Hammers | first=Scott | date=February 5, 2016 | work=The Bend Bulletin | access-date=March 9, 2016}}</ref> business consultant Dan Laschober,<ref name=Laschober>{{cite news | url=http://laschober2016.com | title=Laschober for U.S. Senate | access-date=2019-05-16 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20180808063836/http://laschober2016.com/ | archive-date=August 8, 2018 | url-status=dead }}</ref> Steven Reynolds,<ref name=SReynolds/> and [[Lane County, Oregon|Lane County]] commissioner Faye Stewart<ref name=FStewart>{{cite news | last=Dubois | first=Steven | date=November 11, 2015 | work=KATU | url=http://katu.com/news/local/oregon-county-commissioner-faye-stewart-to-run-for-us-senate | title=Oregon county commissioner Faye Stewart to run for US Senate | access-date=December 3, 2015}}</ref> ran for the Republican nomination. Callahan won the Republican nomination.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Pennsylvania}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Pennsylvania}}
One-term senator [[Pat Toomey]] (Republican) was elected with 51% of the vote in 2010. He was 54 years old in 2016. Toomey ran for re-election.<ref name=tsr/>

[[Everett Stern]], a security intelligence consultant and [[whistleblower]] of the [[HSBC]] money laundering scandal, announced that he would challenge Toomey for the Republican nomination,<ref>{{cite web | last=Stern | first=Everett | date=January 26, 2015 | work=Facebook | url=https://www.facebook.com/EverettSternFan/posts/1045534688793832 | title=After serious consideration, I have made the decision to run for the PA United States Senate seat ... | access-date=April 5, 2015}}</ref> but missed the filing deadline, so Toomey was unopposed in the primary.

Democratic candidates included [[Katie McGinty]], former chief of staff to [[List of Governors of Pennsylvania|Governor]] [[Tom Wolf]] and former [[Pennsylvania Department of Environmental Protection#Secretaries|secretary]] of the [[Pennsylvania Department of Environmental Protection]];<ref name=KatieMcginty/> former Congressman [[Joe Sestak]], who defeated incumbent senator [[Arlen Specter]] (a Democrat turned Republican turned back to Democrat) for the [[2010 United States Senate election in Pennsylvania|2010]] Democratic nomination, but lost to Toomey in the general election;<ref name="wapo">{{cite news | last=Weiner | first=Rachel | url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/blogs/post-politics/wp/2013/05/14/joe-sestak-planning-senate-campaign | title=Joe Sestak planning Senate campaign | newspaper=The Washington Post | access-date=October 22, 2013}}</ref> then-current mayor of [[Braddock, Pennsylvania]], [[John Fetterman]],<ref name=fetterman>{{cite news | last=Maher | first=Chris | url=https://www.wsj.com/articles/mayor-of-struggling-steel-town-enters-pennsylvania-senate-race-1442261628 | title=Steel Town Mayor John Fetterman Enters Pennsylvania Senate Race | work=WSJ | date=September 14, 2015}}</ref> an [[AmeriCorps]] alum and [[Harvard University]] graduate;<ref>{{cite web | url=https://obamawhitehouse.archives.gov/champions/americorps-alums/john-fetterman- | title=John fetterman | access-date=July 25, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20170122002132/https://obamawhitehouse.archives.gov/champions/americorps-alums/john-fetterman- | archive-date=2017-01-22 | via=[[NARA|National Archives]] | work=[[whitehouse.gov]] | url-status=live }}</ref> and small businessman and senate candidate in [[2010 United States Senate election in Pennsylvania|2010]] and [[2012 United States Senate election in Pennsylvania|2012]] Joseph Vodvarka.<ref name=Vodvarka>{{cite news | url=http://www.montcoarea4democrats.com/category/montco-area-democrats/montco-area-democrats/joseph-vodvarka-for-us-senate-2016 | title=Joseph Vodvarka for US Senate 2016 | date=December 28, 2015 | work=Montco Area 4 Democrats | access-date=February 18, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160307161026/http://www.montcoarea4democrats.com/category/montco-area-democrats/montco-area-democrats/joseph-vodvarka-for-us-senate-2016/ | archive-date=March 7, 2016 | url-status=dead | df=mdy-all}}</ref> [[Allentown, Pennsylvania|Allentown]] Mayor [[Ed Pawlowski]] announced his candidacy for the seat but suspended his campaign due to an [[Federal Bureau of Investigation|FBI]] investigation of Allentown.<ref>{{cite news | url=http://www.philly.com/philly/news/politics/20150707_Senate_hopeful_halts_bid__Dems_seek_options.html | title=Pawlowski halts Senate bid, Democrats seek options | work=[[The Philadelphia Inquirer]] | last1=Tamari | first1=Jonathan | last2=Roebuck | first2=Jeremy | date=July 6, 2015 | access-date=July 8, 2015}}</ref> McGinty won the primary and faced Toomey in the general election on November 8, 2016. Toomey defeated McGinty and retained the seat.

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in South Carolina}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from South Carolina}}
Two-term Republican senator [[Jim DeMint]] (Republican) was re-elected with 61% of the vote [[2010 United States Senate election in South Carolina|in 2010]]. He resigned at the start of 2013 to become president of [[The Heritage Foundation]], and U.S. representative [[Tim Scott]] (Republican) of [[South Carolina's 1st congressional district]] was appointed to replace DeMint by Governor [[Nikki Haley]].<ref>{{cite news | url=https://www.nytimes.com/2012/12/18/us/politics/congressman-picked-for-south-carolina-senate-seat.html | work=[[The New York Times]] | first=Jeff | last=Zeleny | title=Rep. Tim Scott Chosen to Replace Jim DeMint as South Carolina Senator | date=December 17, 2012}}</ref>

Scott subsequently won the [[2014 United States Senate special election in South Carolina|special election in 2014]] for the remaining two years of the term. He ran for re-election<ref name=RCRG/> and was a potential Republican vice presidential nominee.<ref name="Farm Team SC">{{cite news | last=Livingston | first=Abby | title=South Carolina Republicans Will Have to Ride the Bench | url=http://www.rollcall.com/news/south_carolina_republicans_will_have_to_ride_the_bench-230854-1.html | access-date=April 2, 2014 | publisher=[[Roll Call]] | date=February 12, 2014}}</ref><ref name=Fix>{{cite news | last=Cillizza | first=Chris | title=Tim Scott and the changing face of the Republican party | url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/blogs/the-fix/wp/2012/12/17/tim-scott-and-the-changing-face-of-the-republican-party | access-date=April 2, 2014 | newspaper=The Washington Post | date=December 17, 2012}}</ref>

Other potential Republican candidates included Congressmen [[Mick Mulvaney]],<ref>{{cite news | last=Jaffe | first=Greg | title=As budget cuts hit S.C., a congressman is surprised at constituents' reactions | url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/national/as-budget-cuts-hit-sc-a-congressman-is-surprised-at-constituents-reactions/2013/05/28/b7566fb6-c7c5-11e2-9245-773c0123c027_story_1.html | newspaper=The Washington Post | date=May 28, 2013}}</ref> [[Jeff Duncan (politician)|Jeff Duncan]], [[Mark Sanford]]; State Senator [[Tom Davis (South Carolina politician)|Tom Davis]]; State Treasurer [[Curtis Loftis]]; and State Attorney General [[Alan Wilson (politician)|Alan Wilson]].<ref name="Farm Team SC"/> [[Darla Moore]] was mentioned as a potential candidate for either party.<ref name="Farm Team SC"/>

On the Democratic side, Pastor Thomas Dixon ran in the general election on November 8, 2016 but was defeated by the incumbent, Scott.<ref name=dixonsc/>

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in South Dakota}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from South Dakota}}
Two-term senator [[John Thune]] (Republican) ran unopposed and was re-elected with 100% in 2010.<ref name=thunerunning/>

Jay Williams, Chair of the [[Yankton County, South Dakota|Yankton County]] Democratic Party, and candidate for the state House in 2010 and 2014, ran for the Democratic nomination.<ref name=siebold/> Other potential Democratic candidates included State Senator [[Bernie Hunhoff]]<ref>{{cite news | last=Lawrence | first=Tom | url=http://sdprairie.blogspot.com/2015/07/will-anyone-take-on-thune-in-2016.html | date=July 1, 2015 | title=Will anyone take on Thune in 2016? | work=Prairie Perspective | access-date=August 27, 2015}}</ref> and filmmaker and former television news producer Sam Hurst.<ref>{{cite news | last=Woster | first=Kevin | date=August 24, 2015 | work=[[KELO-TV]] | url=http://blog.keloland.com/politicsinkeloland/2015/08/24/hurst-for-u-s-senate-in-2016-well | title=Hurst for U.S. Senate in 2016? Well ... | access-date=August 27, 2015 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150826005406/http://blog.keloland.com/politicsinkeloland/2015/08/24/hurst-for-u-s-senate-in-2016-well/ | archive-date=2015-08-26 | url-status=dead }}</ref>

Former U.S. representative [[Stephanie Herseth Sandlin]], [[Sioux Falls, South Dakota|Sioux Falls]] Mayor Mike Heuther, and [[2014 United States Senate election in South Dakota|2014]] nominee [[Rick Weiland]] all declined to run.<ref>{{cite news | last=Tupper | first=Seth | url=http://rapidcityjournal.com/blog/on-background/thune-frolicking-through-another-opponent-less-election-season/article_9cf262e6-a289-11e5-a979-4742a43a7d9b.html | title=Thune frolicking through another opponent-less election season | date=December 14, 2015 | work=[[Rapid City Journal]] | access-date=February 20, 2016}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | date=March 18, 2015 | url=http://rapidcityjournal.com/news/rick-weiland-returns-with-new-nonpartisan-political-group/article_3d941c0e-6cdc-5d1c-a016-d384a6bdf092.html | title=Rick Weiland returns with new nonpartisan political group | work=[[Rapid City Journal]] | access-date=May 20, 2015}}</ref>

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Utah}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Utah}}
One-term senator [[Mike Lee]] (Republican) was elected with 62% of the vote in 2010. He was 45 years old in 2016. He ran for re-election.<ref name=rousing/> State party chair Thomas Wright, former state senator [[Dan Liljenquist]], State Senator [[Aaron Osmond]], Congressman [[Jason Chaffetz]], Congressman [[Chris Stewart (Utah politician)|Chris Stewart]], former [[governor of Utah]] [[Mike Leavitt]], and [[Mitt Romney]]'s son Josh Romney<ref name=compromise>{{cite news | url=http://www.sltrib.com/sltrib/politics/56980406-90/compromise-lee-monson-percent.html.csp | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20131010022923/http://www.sltrib.com/sltrib/politics/56980406-90/compromise-lee-monson-percent.html.csp | title=Most Utahns disapprove of Sen. Mike Lee, want him to compromise | first=Robert | last= Gehrke | newspaper=[[Salt Lake Tribune]] | date=October 10, 2013 | archive-date=October 10, 2013 | access-date=November 8, 2014}}</ref><ref name=backlash>{{cite news | url=https://www.washingtonpost.com/politics/in-utah-tea-party-favorite-sen-lee-faces-gop-backlash-over-government-shutdown/2013/10/22/9754e782-3b25-11e3-a94f-b58017bfee6c_story.html | title=In Utah, tea party favorite Sen. Lee faces GOP backlash over government shutdown | first=Philip | last=Rucker | newspaper=The Washington Post | date=October 22, 2013 | access-date=November 8, 2014}}</ref><ref name=UTFT>{{cite news | last=Livingston | first=Abby | title=Next Utah Races to Watch Are for Senate | url=http://www.rollcall.com/news/next_utah_races_to_watch_are_for_senate-231890-1.html | access-date=April 3, 2014 | publisher=[[Roll Call]] | date=April 2, 2014}}</ref> were mentioned as potential primary challengers, but all declined to run.<ref>{{cite news | last=Canham | first=Matt | url=http://www.sltrib.com/news/2382612-155/mike-lee-could-be-a-lock | title=Mike Lee could be a lock for re-election to the Senate | date=April 12, 2015 | work=[[The Salt Lake Tribune]] | access-date=February 4, 2016}}</ref><ref>{{cite news | last=Roche | first=Lisa Riley | url=http://www.deseretnews.com/article/865626952/Josh-Romney-I-wont-run-against-Sen-Mike-Lee-but-another-Republican-should.html | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150422170541/http://www.deseretnews.com/article/865626952/Josh-Romney-I-wont-run-against-Sen-Mike-Lee-but-another-Republican-should.html | url-status=dead | archive-date=April 22, 2015 | title=Josh Romney: I won't run against Sen. Mike Lee, but another Republican should | work=[[Deseret News]] | date=April 21, 2015}}</ref> Lee ran unopposed at the Utah Republican convention, and was the Republican nominee.<ref name="leeconvention">{{cite news | last1=Price | first1=Michelle | title=Utah Gov. Herbert fails to secure nomination at convention | url=http://www.sunherald.com/news/health/article73583842.html | access-date=April 27, 2016 | publisher=SunHerald | date=April 23, 2016 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20160425100854/http://www.sunherald.com/news/health/article73583842.html | archive-date=2016-04-25 | url-status=dead }}</ref>

Marriage therapist Jonathan Swinton<ref name=Swinton>{{cite news | last=Canham | first=Matt | url=http://www.sltrib.com/news/2847352-155/sen-mike-lees-first-challenger- | title=Sen. Mike Lee's first challenger is marriage therapist Jonathan Swinton, an untested Democrat | date=August 17, 2015 | work=[[The Salt Lake Tribune]]}}</ref> and grocery store clerk [[Misty Snow]], a [[transgender woman]], ran for the Democratic nomination. Snow defeated Swinton by more than 20 percentage points, running to the left of Swinton, criticizing him for supporting limitations on abortion rights. She became the first transgender woman to become a major party's nominee for the Senate.<ref>{{cite news | url=https://www.politico.com/story/2016/06/misty-snow-plowright-transgender-congress-224921 | title=Two transgender candidates named 'Misty' win primaries | last=Nelson | first=Louis | access-date=June 29, 2016 | work=Politico.com | date=June 2016}}</ref>

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Vermont}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Vermont}}
Seven-term [[Democratic Party (United States)|Democratic]] Senator [[Patrick Leahy]] was re-elected with 64% of the vote in 2010. Leahy won re-election in 2016, aged 76.<ref name="McGilvery"/>

[[Scott Milne]], the [[Republican Party (United States)|Republican]] nominee who narrowly lost the [[2014 Vermont gubernatorial election]], ran unsuccessfully against Leahy.<ref>{{cite news | url=http://www.sevendaysvt.com/OffMessage/archives/2015/10/05/scott-milne-considers-challenging-patrick-leahy| last=Heintz | first=Paul | title=Scott Milne Considers Challenging Patrick Leahy | date=October 5, 2015 | work=[[Seven Days (newspaper)|Seven Days]]}}</ref><ref>{{cite web | url=http://www.burlingtonfreepress.com/story/news/2016/05/26/milne-hopes-unseat-sen-leahy/84999986 | title=Milne hopes to unseat Sen. Leahy | work=[[The Burlington Free Press]] | last=Gram | first=Dave | date=May 26, 2016 | access-date=May 27, 2016}}</ref>

{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Washington}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Washington}}
Four-term senator [[Patty Murray]] (Democrat) was re-elected with 52% of the vote in 2010. She ran successfully for re-election against Republican candidate [[Chris Vance (politician)|Chris Vance]].<ref name="Pathe"/> Congressman [[Dave Reichert]] was considered a potential Republican candidate<ref name=Cameron>{{cite news | last=Joseph | first=Cameron | title=Rep. Reichert 'thinking about' run for Senate, governor in Washington | url=https://thehill.com/video/in-the-news/316331-rep-reichert-thinking-about-run-for-senate-governor-in-washington/ | access-date=April 7, 2014 | newspaper=[[The Hill (newspaper)|The Hill]] | date=August 9, 2013}}</ref> but chose to run for reelection.<ref>{{cite news | last1=Brunner | first1=Jim | title=Dave Reichert won't run against Jay Inslee for governor | url=http://www.seattletimes.com/seattle-news/politics/dave-reichert-decides-he-wont-run-against-jay-inslee-for-governor | access-date=November 1, 2015 | newspaper=The Seattle Times | date=October 16, 2015}}</ref>

==Wisconsin==

{{Infobox election

| country            = Wisconsin

| nominee1           = '''[[Ron Johnson]]'''
| party1             = Republican Party (United States)
| popular_vote1      = '''1,479,471'''
| percentage1        = '''50.2%'''

| image2             = Russ Feingold Official Portrait 3.jpg
| nominee2           = [[Russ Feingold]]
| party2             = Democratic Party (United States)
| popular_vote2      = 1,380,335
| percentage2        = 46.8%

| map_image          = 2016 United States Senate election in Wisconsin results map by county.svg
| map_size           = 220px
| map_caption = '''Johnson:''' {{legend0|#ffb2b2|40–50%}} {{legend0|#e27f7f|50–60%}} {{legend0|#d75d5d|60–70%}} {{legend0|#d72f30|70–80%}}<br/>'''Feingold:'''  {{legend0|#a5b0ff|40–50%}} {{legend0|#7996e2|50–60%}} {{legend0|#6674de|60–70%}} {{legend0|#584cde|70–80%}}
| title              = U.S. Senator
| before_election    = [[Ron Johnson]]
| before_party       = Republican Party (United States)
| after_election     = [[Ron Johnson]]
| after_party        = Republican Party (United States)
}}
{{Main|2016 United States Senate election in Wisconsin}}
{{See also|List of United States senators from Wisconsin}}
One-term senator [[Ron Johnson]] (Republican) defeated three-term senator [[Russ Feingold]] (Democrat) with 52% of the vote in 2010.

On May 14, 2015, Feingold announced that he would seek a rematch against Johnson for his former Senate seat.<ref name=Feingold/> Immediately after his announcement, the [[Democratic Senatorial Campaign Committee]] endorsed Feingold's candidacy.<ref>{{cite web | url=http://www.dscc.org/pressrelease/dscc-announces-endorsement-russ-feingold-wisconsin-senate-race | title=DSCC Announces Endorsement Of Russ Feingold In Wisconsin Senate Race | date=May 14, 2015 | access-date=May 16, 2015 | archive-date=May 18, 2015 | archive-url=https://web.archive.org/web/20150518090939/http://www.dscc.org/pressrelease/dscc-announces-endorsement-russ-feingold-wisconsin-senate-race | url-status=dead }}</ref> Businesswoman and [[2014 Wisconsin gubernatorial election|2014 gubernatorial nominee]] [[Mary Burke]] declared that she was not seeking statewide office in 2016.<ref>{{cite web | url=http://host.madison.com/news/local/govt-and-politics/mary-burke-says-she-won-t-run-for-statewide-office/article_7eeb8cc5-7219-5e5f-a54c-ac77806ba762.html | title=Mary Burke says she won't run for statewide office again | date=November 10, 2014 | access-date=November 10, 2014 | work=Wisconsin State Journal}}</ref>

Johnson and Feingold faced each other again, and Johnson again defeated Feingold, in what many observers and pundits considered to be a surprising and uphill victory.<ref name=RJohnson/>
